# Implementation questions and provisional decisions

Gaps found in the spec (revision 549o; revision 549 until 2026-10-12, then 549a, 549b and 549c until 2026-10-15, then 549d and 549e until 2026-10-16, then 549f, 549g and 549h until 2026-10-19, then 549i, 549j and 549k the same day, then 549l until 2026-10-20, then 549m until 2026-10-21, then 549n until 2026-10-25) during implementation. Every row is provisional until the owner confirms it; confirmed rows return to the spec as new D-n decisions in a later export. Code that relies on a row carries a `# Q-n` comment.

Status is one of: `provisional` (a new row), `confirmed` (the owner accepted the provisional decision), `changed (#n)` (the owner chose otherwise; the row's decision is updated and the code follows in issue #n: the row's own ticket when the owner answered during its build, otherwise an issue implemented on its own), or `in spec (D-n, revision r)` (returned to the spec).

| ID | Spec location | Gap | Provisional decision | Rationale | Alternatives | Detecting test | Status |

| Q-2 | §2.3, `[fast]` row | The §2.3 table lists fused-ssim in `[fast]`, but fused-ssim is not on PyPI, and a git-URL dependency would block a PyPI upload (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `[fast]` stays an empty extra; the README says to install fused-ssim from its MIT repository; M6, which first uses it, imports it optionally and names the commit it was tested with | keeps the M18 PyPI release possible; owner, 2026-10-11: the real fix is fused-ssim publishing to PyPI, and until then `[fast]` stays empty with a manual install in the README, as Q-6 does for fused-local-corr on Python 3.14 | `fused-ssim @ git+…@<commit>` in `[fast]`; drop `[fast]` | M18 upload dry run; M6 optional-import test | confirmed |
| Q-3 | §2.3, torch row ("≥2.1, CUDA build") | Package metadata can only name a version range: it can't pick a CUDA version other than the one PyPI's wheel bundles, require CUDA on Windows, or stop a user from installing a CPU build (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `torch>=2.1` in metadata. On Linux x86_64 this resolves to PyPI's default torch, which is a CUDA build (2.14.1 depends on the CUDA 13.0 libraries on Linux; source log #4). The README says so and points to the PyTorch index for other CUDA versions and for Windows. CI installs the CPU wheel on purpose, because its runners have no GPU. A test fails when a GPU is present but torch is a CPU build (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | owner: training needs a CUDA build for performance, and CUDA wheels are available; on Linux the default already is one | per-CUDA extras through indexes (not expressible in metadata); requiring a `+cuXXX` local version (not hosted on PyPI) | `tests/test_environment.py`: on a machine where `nvidia-smi` lists a GPU, `torch.version.cuda` is set and `torch.cuda.is_available()` is true | changed (#1) |
| Q-4 | §2.3 notes, CI scan | §2.3 says CI scans "the resolved environment" and that LGPL needs manual sign-off, but doesn't say which environment CI resolves, how a sign-off is recorded, or how unknown, unrecognised, proprietary and non-commercial licenses and multi-term license expressions are treated (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | Each license string is split into terms on `AND` and `;`; an `OR` choice takes its most favourable option; a distribution takes its worst term; "X or later" is one license, and an exception (`WITH`) is stripped so the base license is judged. **Fail**, no sign-off possible: GPL, AGPL, non-commercial, and a non-MIT albumentations. **Needs sign-off**: LGPL, proprietary, UNKNOWN, unrecognised; the scan fails unless an entry of the committed `scripts/license_signoff.json` matches the name, version and reported license string; entries matching nothing are reported as stale. **Pass**: a fixed list of permissive licenses and MPL-2.0 only. CI scans `.[dev,init]` plus the harness on CPU torch; the CUDA torch environment, with its NVIDIA runtime wheels, is scanned locally and at M18 (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | §2.3 requires every dependency to be Apache-compatible; a sign-off that CI doesn't enforce is no sign-off; proprietary NVIDIA runtime wheels (17 in the CUDA 13.0 torch environment) are installed by the user and never redistributed, so they need a recorded decision rather than an impossible failure; owner: a non-commercial license fails outright | fail on everything not permissive; non-commercial to sign-off like proprietary; scan only `.[dev]` in CI | `tests/test_license_scan.py` | changed (#1) |
| Q-5 | §2.1, `ftgs_harness` row; §3.3 | §2.1 says "separate package", which could mean a separate import package or a separate distribution (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | A distribution `ftgs-harness` with the import package `ftgs_harness`, in `harness/`, with its own pyproject and its own copy of the Apache-2.0 LICENSE, and no dependency on openftgs; its `ftgs-harness` console script arrives with the commands at M16. matplotlib, the owner's decision for the harness plots (`docs/clean-room/milestones.md`, M16, 2026-10-11), is declared now rather than at M16; §2.3 doesn't list it, so the CI license scan checks it (matplotlib 3.11.2 reports the PSF license; its dependencies are BSD, MIT or Apache) (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | §3.3 names the commands `ftgs-harness run` and `ftgs-harness rerun` (spec lines 1277–1278) and says the harness imports neither implementation; a separate distribution matches both and keeps matplotlib out of openftgs installs; declaring matplotlib now puts it under the license scan from the start | subpackage inside the openftgs wheel; declare matplotlib at M16 | `tests/test_skeleton.py`: the harness pyproject names `ftgs-harness` and no openftgs dependency, and the root pyproject packages only `src/`; CI's wheel job: the openftgs wheel has no `ftgs_harness/` files and the harness wheel no `openftgs/` files | confirmed |

| Q-7 | §3 introduction; §3.1 converter checks ("run when the dataset is available; not part of CI"); §2.3 note on pretrained weights; §3.1 U-9 (revision 549o names this row's `weights` marker for the trunk networks, #375) | The spec gates dataset tests and tests that download pretrained weights (RoMa, DINOv2, LPIPS backbones) but names no way to tell the test suite where datasets are or that downloads are allowed (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `dataset` tests run only when the environment variable `OPENFTGS_DATASETS` names a directory holding the converted datasets; `weights` tests run only when `OPENFTGS_ALLOW_DOWNLOADS=1`; otherwise both are skipped with a reason naming the variable (`tests/conftest.py`) | environment variables work the same locally and in CI, need no pytest options, and keep downloads opt-in so CI never fetches weights | pytest command-line options (`--datasets DIR`, `--allow-downloads`); a config file; probing the default cache directories | `tests/test_markers.py`: tests are skipped without the variables and run with them | confirmed |
| Q-8 | E14, D-46; §2.1 rows for `types` and `motion` | No module or signature computes Δt_f; `data` (Foundation) needs it but may not import `motion` (Core); the even-count median convention and duplicate timestamps within one camera are unstated (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Public `openftgs.types.frame_interval(camera_timestamps: Mapping[str, Sequence[float]], time: TimeNormalizer) -> float`. The median of an even count is the mean of the two middle values; medians are taken over the gaps between each camera's distinct raw timestamps, then divided by T_τ once; a camera counts only with at least two distinct timestamps, and the pooled fallback also uses distinct timestamps, so duplicated timestamps neither shrink Δt_f nor block the fallback; 1.0 for a single timestamp; `ValueError` for an empty mapping or a result that is not finite and > 0. `motion`'s share of E14 is the unit conversions of primitive quantities (μt, s = d/T_τ, v = v_τ·T_τ) (owner answer, 2026-10-11; distinct timestamps per camera after the M1 milestone review, F-2, owner answer 2026-10-11) | `types` is the only module every user of Δt_f (`data`, `init`, `model`, `optim`, `train`) may import; the statistical median is the standard definition; dividing once is exact on dyadic data and identical in real arithmetic; keeping duplicates would let a repeated timestamp shrink Δt_f, against D-46's aim | `data` computes it privately (U-18 then tests `data`); `torch.median`'s lower middle value; keeping duplicates within each camera (E14 read literally; the first confirmed decision) | U-18 (even-count, unsynchronized and duplicated-timestamp cases); S-8 (M11) | changed (#10) |
| Q-9 | §1.4 "Time"; §2.1 `TimeNormalizer` | The warning's category, which conversion warns, the case T_τ = 0, input validation and the dtype of tensor results are unstated (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | `to_t` alone emits a `UserWarning` for inputs not inside the closed interval [τ₀, τ₁], NaN included; T_τ = 0 gives t ≡ 0 and τ ≡ τ₀; `ValueError` for bounds that are not finite or τ₁ < τ₀; floating tensors keep their dtype, integer tensors become float64 (D-30), floats and ints return a float. The tensor range check syncs with the host, which is acceptable because the training step passes Python floats (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | Renders call `to_t`, so the render-time warning has one source; `to_tau` maps primitive time centers, which legitimately leave [0, 1] | A custom warning class (one more public name); warning in `to_tau` too; `RuntimeWarning` | U-18 warning case; U-4 (M4) raises no unexpected warning inside the range | confirmed |
| Q-10 | §2.2 checkpoint manifest ("full TrainConfig"); §2.1 `openftgs.config`; §3.3 `harness_config.json` | No function or layout is given for TrainConfig's JSON form; unknown and missing keys are unspecified (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Public `openftgs.config.config_to_dict(cfg: TrainConfig) -> dict[str, Any]` and `config_from_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> TrainConfig`: nested by sub-config (`{"growth": {"n_max": …}}`), tuples written as lists and `None` as null, missing keys take their defaults, an unknown key is a `ValueError` naming its dotted path, ints accepted for float fields, bools rejected for numeric fields, non-finite or overflowing numbers rejected. The same type checks and normalization (ints to floats, lists to tuples) run in every config class's constructor, so `TrainConfig(...)` and `config_from_dict` accept exactly the same values (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | The nesting matches the harness's dotted names; a silently ignored typo would train a different configuration; partial files keep `--config` short | Methods on `TrainConfig`; a flat dotted-key layout; ignoring unknown keys as §2.2 does for file formats | TrainConfig JSON round trip (`tests/test_config.py`); U-21 checkpoint round trip (M10); M16 harness config | confirmed |
| Q-11 | §2.1 `openftgs.config`; §0 notation domains | Only `sh_degree` has a stated range (D-3); the other fields' ranges are implied by their meaning (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Rejected with `ValueError`: `init_opacity`, `dead_opacity`, `prune_opacity` outside (0, 1); `grad_quantile` outside (0, 1]; learning rates, `eps`, `scale_floor`, `scene_scale_factor`, `split_scale`, `grad_threshold`, `ssim_sigma` ≤ 0; loss weights λ_img, λ_ssim, λ_perc, λ_reg and score weights λ_g, λ_o < 0 (0 allowed: Table 5 ablates λ_reg = 0); `betas` outside [0, 1); counts `iterations`, `every`, `sh_interval`, `points_per_frame`, `scale_knn`, `n_max`, `images_per_step`, `log_every` below 1; `ssim_window` below 1 or even; `prefetch` < 0; `start > stop` in growth and relocation; `reg_until` < 0; `score_rate` outside (0, 1]; background components outside [0, 1] (owner answer, 2026-10-11; score weights named after the M1 milestone review, F-1, owner answer 2026-10-11) | The notation gives σ ∈ (0, 1) and positive rates; invalid values would otherwise fail deep inside training with NaN or empty windows | Validate only `sh_degree` and the `Literal` fields; validate at `Trainer` construction instead of in `__post_init__` | Range cases in `tests/test_config.py`; U-21's shortened test calendar (M10) must still validate | confirmed |
| Q-12 | §2.1 `openftgs.motion` row; §2.4 "Kernels"; D-50 | The spec names motion's functions and `temporal_slice` but gives no signatures and no return type (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `__all__` is exactly: `moved_means(means, velocities, times, t)` (E1); `temporal_opacity(times, log_durations, t)` (E4); `effective_opacity(opacity_logits, times, log_durations, t)` (E3); `reg4d_terms(opacity_logits, times, log_durations, t)` (σ·sg[σt] per row, E8); a frozen dataclass `TemporalSlice` with `means_t` [N, 3], `opacities_t` [N], `quats_n` [N, 4], `scales` [N, 3], `opacities` [N] (σ), `reg_terms` [N] and `passes_cull` [N] (bool, σ_eff ≥ 1/255; named after the glossary's "Culling", since "Visible primitive" means positive screen radii, E6; renamed from `visible` after the two-axis review); `temporal_slice(means, times, log_durations, velocities, log_scales, quats, opacity_logits, t, *, impl="auto") -> TemporalSlice`. t is `float \| Tensor` everywhere; stored tensors are passed positionally; the SH concat and `GaussiansAtTime` are left to M3's `at_time`. Since M3, `__all__` also holds the four E14 conversions of Q-27 (`durations_to_normalized`, `durations_to_raw`, `velocities_to_normalized`, `velocities_to_raw`), and `GaussiansAtTime` is defined in `openftgs.model` as the `TemporalSlice` fields plus `sh` (Q-20); `motion`'s `t: float \| Tensor` doesn't conflict with §2.1's `at_time(t: float)`, which passes a float (owner answer, 2026-10-12, after M3, `at_time`'s first use of the slice) | positional tensors keep the compiled function's guards on dtype, device and rank; returning the mask avoids data-dependent shapes; SH is time-independent and not per-row elementwise work | motion defines and returns `GaussiansAtTime`; a NamedTuple; passing a model object (a band violation) | U-1, U-3, U-28; M3's `at_time` | confirmed |
| Q-13 | §2.4 "Kernels" and "Settings" (`PerfConfig.slice_impl = "auto"`); D-50 | What `"auto"` resolves to, what triggers the eager fallback, and what `"cuda"` does before M15 (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `"auto"` is the compiled implementation on CUDA tensors and the eager one on CPU; under `"auto"` only, a failed compilation falls back to eager with a one-time `UserWarning`; a failed compilation is a dynamo or backend error (`TorchDynamoException`) or dynamo reaching its recompile limit for the slice: the slice is compiled with `fullgraph=True`, so reaching the limit raises `torch._dynamo.exc.FailOnRecompileLimitHit` (a plain `Exception`, caught explicitly) and a graph break raises at compile time; the limit stays at dynamo's default, and U-28 and B-3 count recompilations by dynamo's count of unique graphs, since the `fullgraph` path doesn't count frames; the fallback is sticky: every later `"auto"` call in the process runs eager, for every model and device; an explicit `"compiled"` raises on failure; `"cuda"` raises `NotImplementedError` naming D-50, B-1 and M15; any other value raises `ValueError` (owner answer, 2026-10-11: keep eager on CPU; amended by the owner on 2026-10-12, after the triage of #45: reaching the recompile limit counts as a failed compilation) | the reference backend, which runs on CPU, stays plain PyTorch (§2.4 rule 4), and CPU runs and tests skip compile time; rule 4 itself speaks of the reference backend, not of CPU tensors, so the two coincide only while gsplat needs CUDA; an explicit choice should fail loudly. Reaching the recompile limit otherwise made the slice eager with only a log line, no exception and no Python warning, so an explicit `"compiled"` silently became eager and B-1 could time the eager slice; making dynamo raise is the only supported way to detect it, `fullgraph=True` is public, local to the slice and free per call, and the eager fallback is exact (§2.4 rule 2), so a default `"auto"` run keeps going (source log #36) | `"auto"` compiles everywhere; a silent fallback; at the recompile limit, keeping the silent fallback, warning under both values without raising, the process-global `fail_on_recompile_limit_hit` flag (or patching it around every call), reading dynamo's private counters, or raising the limit | U-28; B-3 (M15) sets D-50's final choice; with dynamo's recompile limit set to 1, alternating float32 and float64 inputs makes `"compiled"` raise on the second call (CPU and CUDA), while on CUDA `"auto"` warns once and then matches eager without further warnings (#51) | changed (#51) |
| Q-14 | §2.1 `openftgs.sh` row; E2; §1.1 (C₀, sh0 initialization) | No signatures, and no statement whether the basis normalizes its directions (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `__all__` is exactly: `C0: float = 0.28209479177387814`; `sh_basis(dirs, degree) -> Tensor`, [..., 3] → [..., (degree+1)²] in gsplat 1.5.3's band order and signs (real SH keeping the Condon–Shortley phase; l = 1 is C₁·(−y, z, −x)), normalizing its directions itself, `ValueError` for a degree outside {0, 1, 2, 3} and for a bool or non-int degree; `rgb_to_sh0(rgb)` = (rgb − 0.5)/C₀; `sh0_to_rgb(sh0)` = C₀·sh0 + 0.5 (owner answer, 2026-10-11: confirmed; E2's full color stays a private helper of the render backend, with no public color function in `sh`) | E2 defines d as a unit vector; gsplat normalizes its directions too | require unit input | U-6; the gsplat basis test (GPU) | confirmed |
| Q-15 | §3.1 U-28 pass criterion | "≤ 1e-6 relative" is undefined where σt underflows to exactly 0, and the bool mask can flip at the 1/255 boundary under reordered float32 arithmetic (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | Per output tensor, ‖a − b‖₂ ≤ rtol·‖b‖₂ (relative L2, the measure U-12 names for gradients); `passes_cull` equal except on rows whose eager σ_eff lies within 1e-6 relative of 1/255 (owner answer, 2026-10-11: confirmed) | elementwise relative error is undefined at exact zeros; with N = 10⁶ random rows a boundary flip has a nonzero probability; measured on torch 2.14.1 (RTX 4090 and CPU, N from 1 to 10⁶): worst relative L2 3.6e-8 for values and 1.6e-7 for gradients, while an elementwise criterion with a floor of 1e-6 times the tensor's largest value fails without any bug (2.0e-3 on `means_t`, 1.4e-2 on the `quats` gradient), because fused float32 kernels round differently where results nearly cancel; the small sizes in U-28 (N = 1 to 2,300) keep a few-row error from being averaged away | elementwise rtol with an absolute floor | U-28 | confirmed |

| Q-19 | E14; §2.2 `ftgs-if` (`duration` = s·T_τ, `velocity` = v/T_τ); §1.3 step 7 ("1 for a single timestamp") | With a single timestamp (T_τ = 0) the export gives a zero duration, which the validator rejects, and divides velocities by zero; the import divides by zero too (from #20: M3 · Model and file I/O) | When T_τ = 0, durations and velocities convert with a time unit of 1 instead of T_τ (duration = s, velocity = v), in both directions of `ftgs-if` and in `from_init_points`; positions and time centers are unaffected (t ≡ 0, Q-9) (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | owner: a single-frame model round-trips and validates. It fills a gap rather than departing from the spec: §2.2's formulas are undefined or invalid at T_τ = 0, and the spec already substitutes 1 there (§1.4 "Δt_f := 1", E14, §1.3 step 7), so a unit of 1 is converting with Δt_f; since Q-30 makes single-frame velocities 0, only the duration half has effect (second opinion after M3, 2026-10-12) | refuse to export a single-frame model | single-frame `ftgs-if` round trip; U-19 with one timestamp | in spec (D-70, revision 549a) |

Public attributes `sh_degree`, `time`, `time_unit`. `at_time(t, *, sh_degree=None)` keeps §2.1's `t: float` (normalized time) and raises `ValueError` for L′ > L or L′ < 0. `physical()` returns detached tensors under the `ftgs-if` keys with exactly the values and dtypes `to_exchange` writes (owner answer, 2026-10-12) | model sits below render and density, so both can import E5; Q-12 closed motion's `__all__` to the slice; one definition of the physical values keeps `physical()` and `to_exchange` from drifting | E5 in render or motion; `physical()` keyed by `PARAM_NAMES`; `GaussiansAtTime` limited to the five §1.1 arrays | U-5; `at_time` tests; U-22 | confirmed |

| Q-39 | §3.1 U-7 and U-12 pass criteria; E6; Q-15 | U-7's ≤ 10⁻⁴ and U-12's criteria are undefined where both backends' float32 arithmetic can land on opposite sides of an E6 discontinuity, which changes a pixel or a row by about 1/255 or more with no bug: the α skip at 1/255 (gsplat builds with fast math and evaluates μ′ and the conic in another order), the exclusive stop at T = 10⁻⁴, the extent at the opacity threshold (gsplat's `__logf(σ_eff / float32(1/255))` against the reference's `log(255·σ_eff)`; M4 milestone review, finding 5), a radius one off where e·√Σ′ᵢᵢ is within rounding of an integer, near-equal depths, and the depth cull at near and far (from #44: M5 · gsplat renderer) | Exclusions, not a looser tolerance: pixels and rows that are kept are held to the spec's criteria. Pixels excluded: any primitive's float64 α within 10⁻³ relative of 1/255, any float64 T after a primitive within 10⁻³ relative of 10⁻⁴, or two contributing primitives whose float64 depths are within 10⁻⁵ relative. Rows excluded from the `visible` comparison: float64 e·√Σ′ᵢᵢ within 10⁻⁴ of an integer, or a box edge within 10⁻⁴ px of the image edge. Random scenes keep σ_eff at least 10⁻³ relative from 1/255 and \|z − 0.01\| ≥ 10⁻³. The excluded share is asserted ≤ 1% per scene, and the tests report the largest \|Δ\| outside the mask and the largest excluded share; U-7 also reports how many excluded pixels differ between the backends by more than its 10⁻⁴, so the record shows whether the exclusions ever change a result and whether the margins could be narrowed. First run (2026-10-12, RTX 4090, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, gsplat 1.5.3, both layouts): U-7's largest \|Δ\| outside the mask 3.4·10⁻⁶ and largest excluded share 0.23% of a camera's pixels over the 50 scenes, and none of the 128 excluded pixels differing by more than 10⁻⁴, so on this machine the exclusions changed no result; U-12's worst cosine 0.9999999 or above, worst relative L2 error 2.0·10⁻⁵ (it varies run to run in the last digit, since gsplat's backward accumulates with atomics) and largest excluded share 0.16% over the 20 scenes | redraw scenes until no pixel is near a boundary; compare without exclusions and accept seed-dependent failures | the reported excluded share and largest \|Δ\| outside the mask in U-7 and U-12, and U-7's count of excluded pixels that differ by more than 10⁻⁴ (`tests/test_render_gsplat.py`) | changed (#44) |

`forward(pred, gt)` takes the images above as the caller lays them out, with no mapping common to the networks (each maps its inputs as its row of D-89's table says: HaarPSI clamps the render to [0, 1], a Caffe trunk takes BGR × 255 minus its mean pixel, a DINO network halves and standardizes), the reference detached, and returns the 0-d mean over images; D-68's mapping retired with Q-58, and `LPIPSLoss` left `openftgs.losses` in #392). **`reg4d_loss`** and **`total_loss`** take `t` as the training image's normalized time (§1.4), never a raw timestamp; `reg4d_loss` of an empty model is 0. **`total_loss`:** `it` must be an int ≥ 1 (not a bool); L_reg is active when λ_reg > 0 and (`reg_until` is None or it ≤ `reg_until`); a term whose λ is 0 is not computed and its key is absent for the whole run, while after `reg_until` L_reg is still reported under `"reg"` (unweighted, detached, computed without a gradient) but no longer added to the total (owner answer, 2026-10-12, after M6: confirmed with this amendment, so the key set is fixed for a run and the loss trace D-10 names shows L_reg after a cutoff); keys `"l1"`, `"one_minus_ssim"`, `"perceptual"`, `"reg"` (unweighted) and `"total"` (the weighted sum); a `perceptual` network other than the one `cfg.perc_net` names is a `ValueError` (D-9; until revision 549o, one whose backbone differed), and λ_perc = None is that network's calibrated weight (D-89, Q-249); skipping zero-weight terms saves the perceptual network's pass in λ_perc = 0 ablations (Table 5); the glossary reserves "DSSIM" for (1 − SSIM)/2 (and, until revision 549o, listed unqualified "lpips" under _Avoid_ since the backbone could be AlexNet) | NCHW inputs; Backend "gsplat" on a CUDA device, "reference" otherwise. From `InitPoints`: `from_init_points(points, dataset.time, cfg.init, frame_dt=dataset.frame_dt, generator=<init generator, Q-72>, sh_degree=cfg.sh_degree, n_max=cfg.growth.n_max)`, the dataset's time unit, then the device. From a model: the trainer copies it, as `FreeTimeGaussians` copies the tensors it is given, so the caller's object is never modified or moved; `ValueError` unless its SH degree, time normalizer and time unit equal the configuration's and the dataset's and N ≤ N_max. A read-only property `Trainer.model` returns the model the trainer is training, which `load_state_dict` (and so `from_checkpoint`) replaces (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after M10: changed from training a passed model in place, which made one model reused for two runs start the second from the first one's trained state, and left callers without a handle on the trained model after a load). The optimizer uses S = `dataset.spatial_scale(cfg.scene_scale_factor)`, Δt_f = `dataset.frame_dt` and `adam=cfg.perf.adam`; `LPIPSLoss(cfg.loss.perc_net)` is built once, only when λ_perc > 0. `state_dict()` returns CPU clones: `config_to_dict(cfg)`, the run's time normalizer, S and Δt_f, params, `optim`, `stats`, `rng` and `sampler` (Q-72), the manifest's SHA-256 and the time unit. `load_state_dict(state)` requires `state.config == config_to_dict(cfg)` and the dataset's time unit, adopts the state's time normalizer, S and Δt_f, builds a new model, an optimizer from the state's S and Δt_f (Q-46's note) and `GradStats(N)`, loads them, checks and restores the sampler and generators (Q-72) and the iteration (in [0, T_it]); it builds everything before replacing anything, and doesn't check the manifest hash (Q-70). Divergence: a non-finite mean loss at a log read, or a `model.validate()` failure at a log read or before an event, makes `step` raise `FloatingPointError` naming the iteration; M14 maps it to exit code 3. Notes for M14 (owner answer, 2026-10-13): `load_state_dict`'s configuration equality also covers `PerfConfig`, so resuming with a different performance-only setting (`packed`, `log_every`, …) is refused; `--resume` should decide whether such fields may differ, which relaxing later breaks nothing; divergence is detected at the next log read or event, up to `log_every` iterations late; the backend follows the device, and a `backend` keyword, if ever needed, would be a keyword-only addition. Since #183, `load_state_dict`'s configuration check skips the exact `PerfConfig` fields, which a resume may change (Q-134 as answered; `config.resume_conflicts`, Q-153) (owner answer, 2026-10-18, during the build of #183: this pointer, the decision above otherwise as recorded). Since #388 (revision 549o, #380): for D-89's names the trainer builds `PerceptualLoss(cfg.loss.perc_net)`, when λ_perc is None or positive (Q-249); `LPIPSLoss` for LPIPS's backbones until #392 removes them | §2.4 rule 3 keeps losses on the device except at the reads every `log_every` iterations; a callback's return value gives M14's SIGINT and SIGTERM saves a clean stop between iterations; D-76 already ties gsplat to CUDA; `FloatingPointError` is a built-in `ArithmeticError`, so no new public name and no confusion with Q-26's `ValueError`; CPU state is device-independent like `load_checkpoint`'s | float losses every step (one synchronization per iteration); stopping by an exception; a dedicated `ValueError` subclass for divergence (Q-26 allows one); a `backend` keyword on `__init__` (a §2.1 change); training a passed model in place (the first provisional decision); device tensors from `state_dict` | U-21, U-24, S-10; the construction refusals; `FloatingPointError` after a NaN injected through `Trainer.model`; a passed model left bitwise unchanged; two trainers from one model object giving identical runs; the `until` and callback tests (`tests/test_train.py`) | changed (#97) |

| Q-71 | §1.3 step 7 and "Iteration calendar"; E13 (L_active); D-17, D-18, D-45; §1.3 "Count-control modes" (the ablation's pruning and opacity reset); the windows of `GrowthConfig` and `RelocConfig`; Q-51; Q-53 | U-17 needs a seam for the calendar; the windows are separate config fields; when the ablation prunes and resets opacity, and what an event does with relocation off, are unstated (from #84: M10 · Dataset core and trainer) | `calendar_entry(cfg: TrainConfig, it: int) -> CalendarEntry`, pure; `it` an int (not a bool) in [1, `cfg.optim.iterations`] else `ValueError`. `CalendarEntry`, frozen: `sh_degree` = min(⌊(it − 1)/`optim.sh_interval`⌋, `sh_degree`) (E13); `grow` when it is a multiple of `reloc.every`, `growth.start` ≤ it ≤ `growth.stop` and `growth.mode` ≠ "none"; `relocate` when `reloc.enabled`, it is a multiple of `reloc.every` and `reloc.start` ≤ it ≤ `reloc.stop`; `prune` when `reloc.prune_opacity` is set, it is a multiple of `reloc.every` and inside the growth window; `reset_opacity` when `reloc.prune_opacity` is set, it is a multiple of `OPACITY_RESET_EVERY` (Q-67) and inside the growth window (3,000, 6,000, 9,000, 12,000 and 15,000 by default; owner answer, 2026-10-13, while planning); the property `event` = grow or relocate or prune, the iterations whose statistics are reset. Within an event the trainer runs grow, relocate, prune, reset_opacity in that order, then resets the statistics where relocate didn't run (relocate always resets them, Q-51). The ablation turns relocation off, so the trainer refuses `prune_opacity` with `reloc.enabled` (`ValueError` at construction; after the M10 milestone review) (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after M10: confirmed, with notes: (1) for the next spec export, the reset's growth window goes beyond §1.3's literal "an opacity reset to 0.01 every 3,000 iterations", which names no window, so the export should state it; (2) as Q-67 notes, shortened calendars don't scale the reset's 3,000; (3) the entry doesn't carry the calendar table's last rows: "4D regularization on" is `total_loss`'s `reg_until` (Q-41), and "Final checkpoint and evaluation" at T_it is M14's `ftgs train`; (4) `calendar_entry` itself accepts a configuration the trainer refuses (a pruning opacity with relocation on, reported as both relocating and pruning); the refusal stays in the trainer, since refusing it in `RelocConfig` would change the values Q-11 accepts) | every calendar row comes from the configuration, so shortened calendars (§3.2) follow; mode "none" has nothing to grow; 3DGS resets opacity only while it densifies, so pruning can still remove the primitives that don't recover (owner); the statistics reset follows densifying events, as in D-45 | resets over the whole run (3,000 … 30,000); the ablation switched by `enabled = False` instead of `prune_opacity`; `grow` true in mode "none"; resetting the statistics at a reset-only iteration; refusing `prune_opacity` with `enabled` in `RelocConfig` itself (it would change the values Q-11 accepts); relocating and pruning in one event | U-17's calendar part; the ablation calendar; the trainer test that growth runs before relocation; the refusal of a pruning opacity with relocation on (`tests/test_train.py`) | confirmed |
| Q-72 | §2.2 `rng.safetensors`, `rng.json` and `sampler.json` rows; D-15 ("the trainer's dedicated, checkpointed generator"); D-26; the §2.1 CLI paragraph ("The trainer reads one seed, TrainConfig.seed"); the generator-device note in `docs/modules/density/growth.md` | Which generator growth's draws and the initial subset use; how one seed seeds several generators, and on which device; whether the global generators are seeded; the layouts of `rng` and `sampler` (from #84: M10 · Dataset core and trainer) | Three CPU `torch.Generator` objects, each seeded with the first 8 bytes (little-endian) of the SHA-256 of "openftgs/<role>/<cfg.seed>": "sampler" (the per-epoch permutations), "relocation" (every event draw in event order: growth's E17 offsets, relocation's targets and positions, the score mode's draws) and "init" (`from_init_points`' subset; not checkpointed). The trainer never seeds or draws from the global generators; `state_dict` records and `load_state_dict` restores PyTorch's CPU state, the CUDA states per device (recorded only when CUDA is initialized, restored only when it is and only for recorded device indices that exist, the others skipped without initializing CUDA), Python's `random` and NumPy's global bit generator. `TrainerState.rng`: tensors "sampler", "relocation", "torch_cpu" and "torch_cuda": {"<device index>": …} (`rng.safetensors`, Q-25); "python" (`random.getstate()` as JSON lists) and "numpy" (the bit-generator state as JSON) in `rng.json`. `TrainerState.sampler` = {"epoch" (from 0), "cursor" (images taken from the current permutation), "permutation" (the current epoch's dataset indices of training records)}; the first permutation is drawn at construction and the next when the current one runs out; `load_state_dict` requires a permutation of this dataset's training indices, a cursor in [0, len] and an epoch ≥ 0. Since the two-axis review of #84, the recorded global states are checked on throwaway generators before any is restored (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after M10: confirmed, with notes: (1) the seed derivation is part of reproducibility, since changing it changes every run's draws for a given seed, so it changes only by a logged decision (D-72); (2) growth's split offsets come from the relocation generator before relocation's draws, so the same seed under another growth mode or window gives other relocation draws, as another configuration is another run; (3) for M12, the prefetcher peeks at the next epoch's permutation on a clone of the sampler generator's state, never advancing the stream; (4) for M13, `roma_init`'s per-pair seeds from (seed, slot, A, B) (§2.4) can follow the same hashing pattern; (5) for M15, the relocation generator moves to the GPU only if B-1 shows the copy of the scores matters, as a decision change rather than an exact optimization, since it changes the draws, and checkpoints holding CPU states would need converting). Since #388, the trainer builds its perceptual network under `torch.random.fork_rng(devices=[])`: lpips's trunk, since M10, and the vendored DINOv2 builders initialize weights from the global CPU generator before the publisher's are loaded, which drew from it at every `Trainer` construction with a network; no training number depended on it (`test_building_the_perceptual_network_draws_nothing_from_the_global_generator`) | §2.2 lists exactly a relocation and a sampler generator; CPU states load on any device, so checkpoints move between machines and CPU and GPU runs draw identical targets for identical scores; an event synchronizes anyway, so moving the scores to the CPU costs no extra synchronization; unseeded global generators let U-24 catch any hidden global draw; hashing the role keeps streams distinct for every seed | a relocation generator on the model's device (no copy of the scores); a third generator for growth; seeding the global generators at construction; seeds seed, seed + 1, …; the next permutation drawn eagerly | U-21 and U-24 bitwise; different seeds give different image orders; each epoch takes every training image once (`tests/test_train.py`) | confirmed |
| Q-73 | §2.4 "Settings"; D-50, D-51, D-52, D-53, D-54, D-57; §2.4 rule 2; Q-42's note asking M10 to check fused-ssim's backward under `deterministic` | What each `PerfConfig` field does before its milestone, and how process-global flags are set (from #84: M10 · Dataset core and trainer) | `slice_impl` → `model.slice_impl` (Q-66); `packed` and `precull` → `render` (Q-36, Q-31); `adam` → `build_optimizer` (Q-44); `log_every` → Q-69's read period. `deterministic = True`: during each step `cudnn.benchmark = False`, `cudnn.deterministic = True` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True, warn_only=True)`, each restored afterwards; fused-ssim stays in use, since its backward is deterministic (source log #73); `deterministic = False` leaves the cuDNN flags as they are, and autotuning waits for B-4 (M15, §2.4 rule 2). `perc_precision`: "tf32" and "ieee" set `cudnn.allow_tf32` to True or False during each step, restored afterwards (D-52); the float32 matmul precision is "highest" during each step, restored afterwards, so TF32 never reaches matrix products (D-52; after the M10 milestone review); "bf16" raises `NotImplementedError` at construction (M15). `images_per_step` > 1 raises `NotImplementedError` at construction (D-54, M15). `frame_cache` "gpu" or "mmap" raises `NotImplementedError` at construction (D-51, M12); "auto" and "off" decode the image file at every step; `prefetch` is unused until M12 (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after M10: confirmed, with notes: (1) with `deterministic = False` the cuDNN flags stay as PyTorch has them, so autotuning stays off until B-4 shows a gain (§2.4 rule 2), which costs LPIPS some speed meanwhile; M15 turns it on if B-4 shows the gain; (2) deterministic mode is best effort on the GPU: gsplat's backward pass still accumulates with atomic additions (§2.2), and cuBLAS calls warn unless `CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG` is set before CUDA starts, which only a process entry point can do, so it is M14's `ftgs train` or M15's to decide; (3) `prefetch` is accepted and ignored until M12, not refused like the cache placements, since it only changes speed and has a non-zero default; (4) the refusals go when M12 and M15 build those options, and the frame cache's "auto" then picks a cache instead of decoding every iteration, which changes no result (D-51 is exact)) | setting and restoring the flags around each step leaves the caller's process as it was, and `evaluate` keeps its own (D-52); an explicit error beats silently ignoring a setting; rule 2 turns exact optimizations on only after a benchmark | setting the flags once at construction; autotuning on by default now; ignoring the unimplemented options; fused-ssim off in deterministic mode | after `step()` the global flags equal their values before; `NotImplementedError` for each unimplemented option; a GPU run with `deterministic = True` (`tests/test_train.py`) | confirmed |

It returns the eight `METRIC_NAMES` scene means of the entire images and, when the split has a usable dynamic region, the same means under "dynamic/<name>"; an infinite PSNR stays inf. With `out_dir` it writes `out_dir/METRICS.json`: `metrics_report`'s body with a header in Q-63's key names, `producer`, `model` = {"iteration": it} (a trainer's model has no file; M14 owns `model` for files) and `manifest` = {path, sha256}, as JSON with `allow_nan=False`. Rigs: S-1 the S-10 ring (8 cameras, radius 0.9, elevation 0.3, S = 0.99) plus 1 held-out ring camera at a new azimuth and elevation; the S-2 family and S-3 12 training hemisphere cameras at elevations 20°, 40° and 60° (four azimuths each), the radius solved so that S = 1.00 ± 0.02 (0.93), plus 4 held-out cameras at elevations 25°–55° between them, all at a focal length of 56 px; 64×64 images, the focal length chosen so every trajectory stays in view. S-3 runs 10,000 iterations (changed 2026-10-13 during the M11 build with the owner's approval, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent, after the first measured run at 5,000 failed: max \|μt − μt_gt\| 0.84 frame, 11 of the 12 under 0.5, all still shrinking; durations 0.199, PSNR 51.4 dB. §3.2 shortens the calendar but not the rates, so an exponentially decayed group gets T_it/30,000 of its full-run budget: about 5 frames nominal for `times` at 5,000 (D-20), and Adam moves a primitive whose μt gradient is nonzero in a fifth of the images at about a third of its rate, so about 1.7 frames against a 2-frame shift; 10,000 gives 5× the shift nominally. A diagnostic with a constant `times` rate converged to 0.03 frame at 5,000, so nothing biases μt, and L_reg has no gradient through μt (E8). The row nearest the end of the sequence moves slowest, its footprint past the last frame. S-2 keeps 5,000. As the M11 milestone review noted, S-3's 0.5-frame criterion therefore depends on T_it, which §3.2's row doesn't fix: the next export should state S-3's count). Only S-3's count carries the owner's approval; the rest of this row still awaits confirmation, which its decision issue should say. | Q-82 | §3.2 S-6 ("PSNR with relocation ≥ PSNR without + 1 dB (calibrated)"); E10, E11, D-15, D-16; §1.3 "Relocation"; D-18's rationale (S-11 measures relocation under a fixed count) | The calibrated "+1 dB" is unattainable for S-2's scene under any reading: each ground-truth object is one Gaussian and S-2's initialization already sits on it, while E10 scores only live rows and relocation copies dead rows onto them (E11 splits the target's opacity and scale), never onto uncovered content. Measured at N = 16 (Q-78): with relocation 49.55 dB (CPU) and 50.09 (GPU), without 52.12 and 52.12, a gain of −2.57 and −2.04 dB (−1.89 to −2.15 dB over three GPU runs). Most of that is a confound found by the two-axis review of the #99 branch: E8 averages over all N rows, so in the run without relocation the 8 inert dead rows halve the regularizer's push on the 8 live ones (that run beats S-2's 8 rows alone by 1.9 dB); with λ_reg = 0 the gain is −0.06 dB (60.10 against 60.16 dB, CPU). What remains is relocation itself: the split pairs can't both persist (opacity is the fast variable), the copies die and are moved again, and after the window the moved rows are dead again. A Fable subagent's probe of the N = 8 reading gave +0.4 to +0.8 dB from diffuse low-opacity blobs, near gsplat's run-to-run spread of about 0.4 dB (from #99: M11 · Synthetic-scene recovery) | Keep N = 16 and §3.2's criterion verbatim under `xfail(strict=True)`, so the measurement is recorded every run and a pass would be loud; add the mechanical check that the first relocation event moves all 8 dead rows; the measured gains go to the Calibration data table (owner answer, 2026-10-13, during the M11 build, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent). For the next spec export, the owner's options: S-6's PSNR line reported rather than bounded, with "the first event moves every dead primitive" as a criterion; the fixed-N relocation comparison carried by S-11's growth = "none" row, which D-18 already names; or an S-6 ground truth with under-fitted live content where a gain can be derived | the threshold is calibrated "from analytic expectations", and the analytic expectation here is about 0 (≤ 0 at the default λ_reg, through E8's 1/N); switching to N = 8 would be tuning after a measurement and stays at noise level; an xfail keeps CI green without loosening the criterion | the N = 8 reading; the assertion left failing; a threshold set from the measurement | S-6's two tests (`tests/synthetic/`): the mechanical criteria pass, the PSNR criterion stays a strict xfail. Relocation gains only under a capacity deficit: the paper's ablation of periodic relocation (Table 4, dance1) reports +0.67 dB on the entire image and +1.60 dB on the fastest subsequence, against a capacity-limited control (from #99: M11 · Synthetic-scene recovery) | S-6's ground truth is S-2's 8 objects each followed by two companions (committed as its own ftgs-if file, seed 1234): a companion shares the object's motion, time, duration, rotation and opacity, sits CLUSTER_OFFSET = 1.5 × the object's largest scale away in a uniform random direction, with CLUSTER_SCALE = 0.8 × its scales and a uniform random color in [0.2, 0.9]. The initialization stays S-2's (one live row per object, at its center, at a seeded training frame, v = 0) plus 8 dead rows away from the objects (Q-78; their clearance of 5 × 0.035 is measured from S-2's 8 tracks, and companions sit at most 1.5 × 0.035 from their object, so every dead row stays at least 3.5 scales from every companion), N = 16; λ_reg, the calendar and the criteria are unchanged, "+1 dB" asserted (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after the M11 milestone review, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent). For the next export: S-6 reads "S-2's objects as clusters of three Gaussians, initialized as S-2 …" | the deficit's sign and size follow from geometry before measuring: at focal length 56 px and radius 0.93 a scale of 0.035 is about 2 px, so companions 1.5 scales out are about 3 px apart and one row per cluster can't resolve them, while 16 rows for 24 Gaussians can; relocation's copies land within a scale of their target (D-15), which here is where the unfitted companions are. The parameters are those of the advisory probe that showed the design's feasibility (fixed before that probe's run, though after S-6's first failure, so post-hoc in form; the first and only seed probed), and the committed test re-measures it: +8.69 dB on the CPU and the GPU | S-6 left on S-2's content under a strict xfail (Q-82); λ_reg = 0 in both runs (−0.06 dB on S-2's content, +0.5 dB on the clusters); the N = 8 reading (+0.4 to +0.8 dB, noise level); a pruned control (−0.13 dB); S-11's growth = "none" row, which reports but doesn't bound | S-6's two tests (`tests/synthetic/`): the gain criterion asserted; the mechanical criteria (N constant, the first event moves all 8 dead rows, no dead row after an event) | in spec (D-87, revision 549i) |

| Q-99 | D-51; §2.4; `PerfConfig.frame_cache` and `prefetch`; Q-72 note 3; Q-73 note 4 | How "auto" chooses a placement and when, and how the prefetcher meets the sampler (from #109: M12 · Dataset converters and frame cache) | On a CPU device "auto" is mmap. On CUDA the trainer's iterations decode their PNGs as before until "auto" decides: after the iteration that follows the first at the last of the training images' sizes to appear, the second when they all have one size (Q-232, #348; the second whatever the sizes from #341, Q-225; the first until then); then "gpu" is chosen when `size_of` ≤ ½·(free − reserve) − growth (D-51: "half the GPU memory that is free after a warm-up iteration, minus what growth to N_max will add"; ½·(free − growth) in the first build, changed after the two-axis review as the more literal and more conservative reading; ½·free − growth until #325), else "mmap", with free = `mem_get_info` free + (reserved − allocated), reserve = reserved − allocated, the renderer's and the losses' working memory that the iteration left in the allocator (Q-225), and growth = (n_max − N)·4·(4P + 2) bytes, P the floats per row. A cache directory that can't be created makes "auto" fall back to "off" with a `UserWarning`, on CUDA too once "auto" decides; explicit placements raise `ValueError` (Q-26). The prefetcher has depth `prefetch`: on CUDA a pinned ring, a side stream and an event per buffer; on CPU a reader thread; 0 reads synchronously. `take` returns `uint8.float() / 255`, bitwise equal to `load_image`; on CUDA the divisor is a 0-dim device tensor, since there `x / 255.0` with a Python float multiplies by the reciprocal and differs from the CPU's division (measured during the build). The sampler's private `peek(k)` draws the next epoch's permutation on a clone of the generator's state; `load_state_dict` rebuilds the queue (owner answer, 2026-10-15, during the build: confirmed; note for M15: B-6's run should also record peak GPU memory at N_max with the cache on the GPU, to confirm or tighten the "auto" rule, whose growth estimate leaves the renderer's temporary buffers to the ½ margin; until then `frame_cache="mmap"` is the safe setting at the edge of GPU memory) (owner decision, 2026-10-21, after M17's B-6 on studio, #317: changed — `margin_used` reached 1.017 at N_max = 3M and studio's training peaked within 524 MiB of the card, with no measured gain over mmap, so "gpu" also requires room for the renderer's and losses' working memory at N_max; the rule and its estimate are set in #325) | exact (§2.4 rule 2); conservative about memory | decide at construction; prefetch for the GPU placement too | U-30 | changed (#325) |

each entry is one shard with its own dense matcher, so repeating an entry simulates shards on one GPU. `matcher` None means RoMa per `cfg.model`; with a matcher, `model`, `coarse_res`, `upsample_res` and `upsample_preds` are unused. `DenseMatcher` is a Protocol, `__call__(image_a, image_b) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor]`: images [H, W, 3] float32 in [0, 1] on the CPU, as `load_image` returns them; it returns a warp [M, 4] of (x_A, y_A, x_B, y_B) in RoMa's normalized coordinates (±1 the image's outer edges) and a certainty [M] in [0, 1]. `roma_provenance(cfg) -> dict[str, str]` (Q-107). `knn_velocities(points [N, 3], next_points [M, 3], dt: float) -> [N, 3]`: v = (next[nn(i)] − pᵢ)/dt with the exact nearest neighbour, ties to the lower index, in float64 (tiled brute force on explicit coordinate differences on CUDA; SciPy's cKDTree on the CPU, candidates re-sorted by (distance, index), more neighbours queried while the last two distances are equal), returning `points`' dtype; `ValueError` for M = 0 or a dt that is zero or not finite. `triangulate_dlt(x1 [..., 2], x2 [..., 2], P1, P2) -> [..., 3]`: pixels in §1.4's continuous convention, P [3, 4] or broadcastable [..., 3, 4], the 4×4 linear system solved by SVD in float64 and dehomogenized; | Q-119 | §3.2 S-4 and the shared setup; Q-75, Q-79, Q-80 | S-4's scene, sampling, frames, schedule and where PSNR is measured are unstated (from #133: M13 · Initialization) | Three spheres translating linearly; ground-truth Gaussians on a Fibonacci lattice with long durations; 12 hemisphere training cameras plus held-out cameras; 10 frames; an `s4` seed and a committed ground-truth file. Init points: one fixed, jittered lattice per sphere, other than the ground truth's, translated to each frame with a displacement per frame below half the lattice spacing; velocities from `knn_velocities`, with a backward difference on the last frame; frame ids the frame index; durations left to `from_init_points`. Both inits train from the same `InitPoints` (velocities kept, or None) under the same budget, the shortened calendar (Q-75) and growth "none"; PSNR at the held-out cameras on the training frames; velocity error as Q-80 measures it. CPU and GPU, with a CPU target under 3 minutes, else the CPU variant becomes GPU-only as S-5 is; the measured PSNRs go in the Calibration data table. Within this, the build chose: spheres of radius 0.1, each with a step per frame drawn uniformly in 0.018–0.025 in a uniform random direction and its center at the sequence's middle time uniform within 0.2 of the origin, drawn again until its surface stays in view of all 16 hemisphere cameras (the 12 training and 4 held-out ones) and its surface at least 0.06 from the other spheres' at every frame (seed `s4.ground_truth` 1401); ground truth of 80 Gaussians per sphere on a Fibonacci lattice, sharing the sphere's velocity, with isotropic scale 0.45 × the lattice's smallest spacing, opacity 0.9, duration 10 × the sequence span (span 9/30 s), time centers at the sequence's middle time (τ = 4.5/30 s, between frames 4 and 5) and uniform random colors in [0.2, 0.9] at SH degree 0. Init points: a fixed 14-point Fibonacci lattice per sphere under a uniform random rotation, each point jittered by at most 0.05 of the lattice's smallest spacing and put back on the sphere (seed `s4.init` 1402), translated to each of the 10 frames (N = 420) and rounded to float32 before E15 runs on them; each takes the color of the nearest ground-truth Gaussian on its sphere, standing in for colors taken from the images. Training: 2,000 iterations, with relocation and λ_reg at their defaults, SH degree 3 and λ_perc = 0 (Q-79); PSNR the mean over the 4 held-out hemisphere cameras at the 10 training frames. The velocity error is Q-80's relative vector error ‖v − v_gt‖/‖v_gt‖ against the velocity of the point's sphere, in world units per τ, its median over the 420 init points asserted ≤ 5 % (S-8's single number, which bounds the relative magnitude error). S-4 runs on the CPU too, in about 76 s on a near-idle machine (settled by the build of #138, 2026-10-16) Growth "none" departs from §3.2's shared setup, which lists only S-1 to S-3, S-7 and S-8 as growth-off scenes: it is this row's departure, for the next export to state (as Q-78 states it for S-5), so that both runs keep N = 420 and "the same budget" is the same primitive count (owner answer, 2026-10-16, after the milestone review: confirmed, with the growth departure and the rationale's two additions recommended by a Fable review) | Material points persist across frames, which "displacement < half the sampling spacing" implies; the PSNR comparison follows Table 4's direction; 2,000 iterations because E15's lead over the zero-velocity init shrinks as both runs converge (scratch probes on other draws of this scene gave about 0.9–1.6 dB at 1,000–2,000 iterations and about 0.5 dB at 3,000–5,000, the CPU and the GPU within 0.01 dB), and 2,000 keeps the CPU variant under its 3-minute target The velocity half can't fail for a correct E15 (median 2.86e-7, largest 1.03e-6: float32 rounding of the positions); it carries §3.2's precondition check on the committed scene (the largest step at 0.608 of half the spacing), E15 on both k-NN paths (cKDTree and tiled CUDA) at the scene's scale, and the init points the PSNR half trains from, while E15's wiring inside `roma_init` (dt in raw units, the backward difference, slot times) is U-19's and the PSNR half carries the method's value. Resampled points are incompatible with the row, not just harder: "displacement < half the sampling spacing" is exactly the condition under which a persisting point's nearest neighbour is its own moved copy, and points drawn independently each frame on these spheres (14 to 2,000 per frame, step 0.45 × the spacing) give median relative vector errors of 200 % to 7,400 %, never near 5 % (Fable review, 2026-10-16) | points resampled independently per frame; points made by `roma_init` | S-4 | confirmed |
| Q-120 | §2.1 init block (`knn_velocities`, `triangulate_dlt`); Q-106; E15; §1.3 step 3; D-56 | Q-106 gives `knn_velocities` its errors for M = 0 and for dt, but not what malformed shapes, integer dtypes, tensors on two devices, non-finite coordinates or N = 0 do; `triangulate_dlt` has no stated errors and no output dtype, and "degenerate rows come out non-finite" doesn't say which rows are degenerate, while `torch.linalg.svd` makes a whole CPU batch raise on one NaN and returns finite vectors for non-finite rows on CUDA (source log row 129) (from #135: M13 · knn_velocities and triangulate_dlt) | `knn_velocities` also raises `ValueError` when `points` isn't [N, 3] or `next_points` isn't [M, 3], when either isn't floating point, when they sit on two devices, or when a coordinate isn't finite; N = 0 gives an empty [0, 3]. `triangulate_dlt` raises `ValueError` when `x1` or `x2` isn't [..., 2], `P1` or `P2` isn't [..., 3, 4], the batch shapes don't broadcast, an input isn't floating point, or the inputs sit on two devices. It returns float64, shape broadcast(batch shapes) + [3], on the inputs' device. The solver does no filtering: a row with a non-finite input comes out non-finite (NaN, the other rows being solved as usual), and so does a row whose null vector has an exactly zero homogeneous coordinate h₄ (rays that meet only at infinity; the division gives inf or NaN). Every other row comes out finite, a rank-deficient system with h₄ ≠ 0 included: near-degenerate rays (near-parallel, whose rounded h₄ is tiny but not 0) give a finite, far point (about 10¹⁵ away, source log row 129), which `roma_init`'s D-22 filters (depth, reprojection, angle) reject (the owner asked the advisor, Fable 5.1, for a recommendation on #135's question, 2026-10-16: no threshold inside the solver, since D-22's angle filter is the spec's rejection of near-parallel rays; this corrects Q-106's "degenerate rows come out non-finite"; confirmed by the owner, 2026-10-16, after the milestone review) | An error at the call instead of an `IndexError`, or a CUDA device-side assert, deep inside: a non-finite query point has no nearest neighbour. float64 keeps the solve's precision for `roma_init`'s filters, and Q-111 rounds positions to float32 only after them. Solving the finite rows alone keeps one bad match from aborting a batch on the CPU or passing as a plausible finite point on CUDA | PyTorch's own errors (`RuntimeError`, `IndexError`); `triangulate_dlt` returning `x1`'s dtype; a rank or σ₃ test that flags near-degenerate rows (filtering, which Q-106 rules out); NaN velocities for non-finite points | `tests/test_init.py`: the `ValueError` tests of both functions, the DLT's output dtype, the non-finite DLT rows | confirmed |

S-11 runs three: (1) full init, growth "gradient"; (2) each sphere's local x ≥ 0 half only, split by a vertical plane so the cameras, at azimuths all around, see both halves alike, growth "gradient"; (3) run 2's init plus random primitives of opacity 0.01, uniform in the spheres' bounding volume, random colors, up to run 2's final N, growth "none". Earlier wheels (0.2.2 `torch>=2.7.1`, 0.2.4 unpinned) were compiled against older torch builds. Tested on 2026-10-16 (source-log rows 146 and 147): on Python 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13 a fresh resolution of `.[init]` gives fused-local-corr 0.3.211 and torch 2.11.0, three minor releases behind the newest, 2.14.1, which the project is tested on; with torch 2.14.1 required, the resolver falls back to fused-local-corr 0.2.4, whose extension fails to load with it (`libcudart.so.12`: built for CUDA 12, while torch 2.14.1 ships CUDA 13); no release matches torch 2.14 (owner answer, 2026-10-16, during the build of #2: changed, drop it and build from source; the §2.3 wording for a later export is in #189) | `[init]` must never change the user's torch: a downgrade changes rendering and training numerics and leaves the release the project is tested on; a wheel that can't load gives no speed-up while looking installed; one build-from-source instruction serves every Python, as Q-6 already does on 3.14 | keep `>=0.2.2` and let it pin torch (2.11.0 today, moving with every fused-local-corr release); leave as is and document the downgrade; an exact pin of 0.2.4 (doesn't load with torch 2.14.1) | `tests/test_skeleton.py`: `[init]` lists no fused-local-corr; a resolution of `.[init]` on 3.11–3.13 keeps the newest torch (M18's install matrix); U-38 and B-8 with and without the fused kernel, with a source build (no milestone runs this yet: B-8 is M15's and M17's) | in spec (D-85, revision 549h) |

| Q-154 | §2.1 CLI paragraph on METRICS.json; §2.2 checkpoint contract; Q-136 (as answered: "the SHA-256 of the bytes as read"); D-83 | `ftgs eval` reads MODEL to score it and reads it again to hash it; a model that changes in between, as `RUN_DIR/checkpoint` does at every periodic save of a run in progress (D-83), would be named by a hash of bytes other than those scored, or vanish between the two reads (raised by the spec review of #184) | `ftgs eval` hashes MODEL before reading it and again after; if the two hashes differ, the command is exit 2, naming the model as changed while it was read, and writes nothing; an `OSError` while hashing is exit 2 too (Q-142). Only `ftgs eval` hashes; `ftgs render` and `ftgs export` don't read MODEL twice. When the read itself fails, the hash is taken again, and a hash that differs from the first, or can't be taken, gives the same changed-while-read message instead of the reader's, so a save that tears the read isn't reported as a damaged model; an unchanged hash keeps the reader's message (owner answer, 2026-10-18: changed, the message for a torn read added; the code follows in #209) | the hash must name what was scored, which is the point of Q-136's change; a second read can't otherwise be tied to the first, and io's readers return no digest; an evaluation of a run in progress is rare, so refusing it costs little | hash once after reading (the first build); have `load_checkpoint` and `read_exchange` return the digest of what they read (an io API change) | `tests/test_cli.py`: a checkpoint whose `manifest.json` changes while `ftgs eval` reads it is exit 2 with no METRICS.json | changed (#209) |

The parameters, fixed from analysis before any measured run: S-9's committed scene and seeds as they stand (#185's three textured spheres of radius 0.16, 16 training and 4 held-out cameras, 20 frames, 128×96), nothing chosen for S-5; S-9's surface-sample init, 300 lattice points per sphere and frame, N = 18,000, with `from_init_points`' defaults (opacity 0.1, D-5; duration Δt_f, D-21; per-frame 3-NN scales, D-25, footprints of several pixels); growth "none", so both runs keep N = 18,000 and the share compares like with like (Q-78), relocation at its default; S-9's calendar (T_it = 7,000, shortened, λ_perc = 0); λ_reg = 0 against 10⁻² (E8, D-10), the only difference; GPU only, through the `Trainer` API, the two runs shared by two tests in `tests/synthetic/test_end_to_end.py`. Measured: the share of rows with σ > 0.9 over all N (criterion 1, asserted lower with regularization); the held-out PSNR over the 4 held-out cameras × 20 frames, E16's clamp (criterion 2, asserted not lower by more than 0.5 dB); recorded beside them the sphere-coverage PSNR (Q-138), N at the end of both runs (asserted equal), and that the regularized run reports a nonzero L_reg at every iteration (asserted, as M11's S-5). Expected outcome: criterion 1 holds by a clear margin: without E8 nothing pushes the surface rows' opacities down while the images ask for full coverage, and with it the rows behind the front layer, which get almost no image gradient, drop steadily under Adam; the push per row is far weaker than on M11's scene (1/N about 9 times smaller, and D-21's one-frame duration gates it to roughly an eighth of the iterations), but Adam normalizes its size. Criterion 2 holds, \|ΔPSNR\| ≤ 0.5 dB with its sign uncertain: inside a dense surface what lies behind a front primitive is more textured surface, not black, so Q-81's σ·c degeneracy survives only at silhouettes against black, a small share of the pixels (the spheres cover about 16 % of the held-out pixels); S-9's run-to-run spread is about 0.1 dB, well under the margin. Repetitions: a first call, 10 warm-up calls, then the medians of the timed calls; B-1 times windows of training iterations and reports means beside the medians (Q-185); B-8 runs once, without a warm-up. B-1's budget: an upper bound, T_it × the iteration time at 3M plus the default calendar's number of events × the event time, ≤ 3,600 s, labelled as a bound (`kind: "bound"`); Q-185 defines the two times. It bounds N, every iteration being charged at the case's N, and the number of events, each charged as what an event at the cap adds, where growth computes its candidates but adds no rows (Q-185); it doesn't bound the rasterization cost of trained content, the cost of an event that adds rows or has real relocation, or the spread between runs, so a pass is not evidence that the one-hour target is met. B-7: the pose of the first camera with test images, else of the first camera (kitchen's cam00), fx and fy both scaled by 1080/H with H its height (1014 on kitchen), so pixels stay square and the vertical field of view is kept, the principal point at (960, 540), 1920×1080; Q-194 defines the camera (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed; B-7's camera brought into line with Q-194's, which the code follows, where the first wording scaled fy alone; the bound's reach and M17's measure of the budget added; the details referred to Q-181, Q-185 and Q-194; the events' charge corrected with Q-185's answer, an event at the cap rather than one with growth) | measurable on one GPU and repeatable; the real one-hour run is M17's; initialization and the frame cache's build are done once per scene, as conversion is | three full training runs; models filling a volume; the budget on iterations 1 to T_it alone; initialization counted in it | the bench tests; the Calibration rows; M17's timed default run on kitchen | confirmed |

| Q-171 | §3.1 U-29; §2.4 rule 3 | Which frames count as "inside the gsplat render call" (from #220: M15 · Performance architecture) | A synchronization warning passes when, on the training thread, it happens inside one of `render`'s gsplat stage calls (its innermost frame is gsplat's, and its stack runs through `_render_gsplat` into a gsplat function, the first frame past torch's own being gsplat's) or, under `precull="compact"`, is `render`'s kept-row selection (`_kept_rows`); or when, on the autograd device thread, its innermost frame is the backward of one of the gsplat autograd Functions that `render`'s stage calls build (`_FullyFusedProjection`, `_FullyFusedProjectionPacked`, `_SphericalHarmonics` and `_RasterizeToPixels`, in gsplat 1.5.3's `gsplat/cuda/_wrapper.py`), not, say, gsplat's optimizers. A warning on any other thread fails. A warning that reaches only stderr (gsplat's direct calls into its compiled module, which never become Python warnings) is charged to the Python frame that called the extension; a warning replayed from the autograd engine's device thread fails, whoever raised it (the build of #222; narrowed by PRD #220's review, 439064a, where the first wording passed any innermost frame in gsplat's files) (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed, with the decision as the review narrowed it; revision 549l's U-29 states its core, and the stderr and replay rules stay in this row) | rule 3 allows exactly these; a frame anywhere in gsplat's package would also pass gsplat code `render` never calls, such as its Adam; without the stderr route an unpacked render's one synchronization, the tile intersection's, is not seen at all; a replayed warning has lost its origin, so it is refused rather than guessed, and none occurs with gsplat 1.5.3; the prefetch worker's event wait is outside what the debug mode reports and runs off the training thread | gsplat's files only, which fails with compaction; any innermost frame in gsplat's files on either thread (the first wording); replayed warnings accepted | U-29 (`tests/test_synchronization.py`), with the recorder's test of the three routes | confirmed |

| Q-176 | §2.4 "Threads and processes" | The intra-op thread setting at startup is not built, and the spec doesn't say where it goes (from #220: M15 · Performance architecture) | `ftgs`'s `main()` sets PyTorch's intra-op threads once, after parsing, to the number of physical cores among the CPUs the process may run on: the distinct (`physical_package_id`, `core_id`) pairs of the CPUs in its affinity set (`os.sched_getaffinity(0)`), unless `OMP_NUM_THREADS` or `MKL_NUM_THREADS` is set; the library leaves the setting alone. Where the topology can't be read, the fallback is the number of CPUs in the affinity set, and without `os.sched_getaffinity` (not Linux) `os.cpu_count()`. CPU quotas (cgroup `cpu.max`), which leave the affinity set whole, aren't taken into account. Measured on the i9-12900 (#224 and the Q-176 discussion): unrestricted, PyTorch's default is already 16, the physical cores, so the setting changes nothing there; it matters on builds whose default differs (owner answer, 2026-10-19: changed — the place in `main()`, the variables' rule and the library leaving the setting alone confirmed; the count restricted to the affinity set, since PyTorch's default follows it and the first wording, counting the whole machine's cores, raised it: under `taskset -c 0-3` (2 physical cores) from PyTorch's 4 to 16, under `taskset -c 0,1` (1 core) from 2 to 16) | "at startup" belongs to the entry point, as with Q-131; the CPUs a process may use are those of its affinity set (SLURM, `docker --cpuset-cpus`, `taskset`), and counting others can only oversubscribe them | set it in the package's `__init__`; the whole machine's physical cores (the first wording); min(the machine's physical cores, the CPUs in the affinity set), which gives PyTorch's own default under `taskset -c 0-3` and runs two threads on a core | the CLI thread test; a command run under an affinity set of one and of two physical cores' CPUs sets 1 and 2 | changed (#274) |

| Q-180 | §2.1 `ftgs bench` and the exit codes; §3.4 ("JSON with the GPU model, driver, CUDA, PyTorch and gsplat versions"); Q-166; Q-130 | Q-166 fixes BENCH.json's top-level layout and the exit codes, but leaves open what `command`, `case`, `figure` and `kind` hold; the scene's `cameras`, `frames`, `width` and `height`; where the driver's version comes from, PyTorch having no public accessor; what "the commit with a dirty flag" is for an installed package; the order of the checks; and what a benchmark whose ticket hasn't landed does without CUDA (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | `command` is the command line as a list, `["ftgs", "bench", SUBCOMMAND, SCENE, "--out", OUT]` and `--model MODEL` when given, the paths as typed. A case is `{"case": {parameter: value}, "figures": {name: value}}` (B-3: `{"impl", "n"}`, and `{"impl", "resizes"}` for the recompilations), times in seconds (names ending `_s`) and memory in bytes. A budget entry names its `case` too, beside Q-166's keys; its `figure` is a key of that case's figures, and its `kind` is `budget` (a §3.4 budget), `bound` (an upper bound standing for one, as Q-168's for B-1) or `limit` (another spec limit checked on the run: U-28's 2 recompilations in B-3, U-8's 1e-6 in B-2's spot check, D-50's 10 % slice share in B-1); `pass` is `value` at most `limit` for the bounds and limits, at least `limit` for B-7's ≥ 450 FPS (since #351, B-7's calibrated floor: Q-239) and strictly under it for B-6's "< 1%", and never changes the exit code (Q-166). The scene's `path` is absolute, `sha256` that of the manifest's bytes, `cameras` the cameras with images, `frames` the most images any one camera has, `width` and `height` the first training image's; `date` is the start of the run in UTC, ISO 8601 to the second. The environment block holds `gpu` (`name` of the current device and `count`; null and 0 without CUDA), `driver` (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=driver_version`, then `kmd_version`; null when nvidia-smi can't answer; source log 162), `cuda` (`torch.version.cuda`), `cudnn` (`torch.backends.cudnn.version()`), `torch`, `gsplat`, `fused_ssim` (null when absent) and `python`, and also `openftgs` and `cpu` (the CPU's model, for figures bound by the host); `commit` and `dirty` come from git in the folder of the imported package, only when its `__init__.py` is a tracked file there (not for a wheel inside a checkout's `.venv`), `dirty` meaning that tracked files differ from the commit, both null otherwise. The checks run in this order: `--model` on a subcommand that doesn't take it (a usage error, exit 2), the scene (exit 2), CUDA except for `convert` (exit 2), MODEL (exit 2); then the command makes BENCH.json's folder (exit 2 if it can't, before running), runs the benchmark, and replaces the file atomically (Q-126) (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed, with the row brought up to what the code does: Q-130's stub, retired by #225 to #235, dropped from the order of checks; `pass`'s direction and the other limits added) | everything a later comparison needs is in the file, keyed for Q-170's comparison by benchmark, case and figure; the dirty flag guards against numbers from uncommitted code; a missing CUDA is an environment error whether the benchmark is built yet or not | `command` as one string; cases as strings such as "compiled/n=100000"; `frames` as the distinct training timestamps, which counts every image of an unsynchronized rig; pynvml for the driver; untracked files counting as dirty | `tests/test_bench.py`: the layout and environment test and the exit-code tests; `tests/test_cli.py`: the test that replaces Q-130's | confirmed |
| Q-181 | §3.4 setups; Q-168 ("rows on a background sphere and 32 random spheres around the scene's centre, scales from the rows' spacing with the normal axis at 1/5, σ ~ U[0.3, 0.95], SH degree 3, durations set for the target active fraction, seeded"); D-27 | Q-168 leaves open what "the scene's centre" is, the spheres' sizes and places, the background sphere's radius and share of the rows, the factor between spacing and scale, the motion, the colours, the times and durations that give "the target active fraction", and the order of the rows. D-27's centroid of the training camera centres is no centre of the content on a forward-facing rig: on kitchen it lies about 2.6 units behind the cameras' convergence point (S = 1.60), and spheres around it would leave 92 to 98 % of the rows outside each camera's image (source log 163) (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | The centre is the point nearest every training camera's optical axis, by least squares, with a ridge of 10⁻⁶ × the trace of the normal matrix toward the point S ahead of the camera centres' centroid along the cameras' mean axis, which matters only where the axes are parallel. The view radius ρ is the smallest, over the training cameras, of the camera's distance to the centre times the tangent of its narrower half field of view. ⌊N/4⌋ rows lie on a static background sphere about the centre, of radius twice the largest camera distance, so it encloses every camera; the others on 32 spheres, their centres uniform in the ball of radius ρ/2 about the centre and their radii U[0.05, 0.15]·ρ, each row drawn to a sphere with probability ∝ its area. Each sphere moves at its own velocity, a uniform direction at a speed U[0, 0.25]·ρ per unit of normalized time, its centre at its drawn place at t = ½; each row sits at a uniform direction on its sphere as the sphere is at the row's μt, so E1 keeps it on the moving sphere. Tangent scales are the spacing √(4πr²/n) of the n rows on a sphere of radius r, the normal axis 1/5 of it, along the sphere's normal; σ ~ U[0.3, 0.95]. For an active fraction f < 1, μt ~ U[−r, 1 + r] with r = f/(2(1 − f)), and s = r/√(2 ln 255σ): a row passes the cull (D-32) while \|t − μt\| ≤ r, so at any t in [0, 1] a share f of the rows does, in expectation; for f = 1, μt ~ U[0, 1] and s = 10. Colours are per sphere U[0.2, 0.8] per channel (per row U[0.3, 0.7] on the background), plus N(0, 0.05²) per row, clamped to [0, 1], as sh0; shN ~ N(0, 0.02²). Everything is drawn in float64 from one generator on the model's device, seeded, then the rows are shuffled and rounded to float32, so a prefix is a smaller model of the same kind. A scene with a single timestamp is exit 2 (D-71 leaves no row inactive), as is one with fewer than two distinct training camera centres, which has no S (D-27) (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed, with the scene without S added, which the code already refused) | the content must be in view of every camera, on ring and forward-facing rigs alike, for B-1, B-2 and B-7 to time what a camera renders: the convergence point is a ring rig's centre and a forward-facing rig's look-at point, and the view radius sizes the content by what the cameras see, where S measures the rig's spread; activity uniform over [0, 1] matches training, which samples every frame; shuffled rows make B-3's growing prefixes resizes as at growth events | D-27's centroid as the centre, as the ticket's pointer reads; sizes from S; an active fraction exact at one probe time only; a background plane facing the cameras | the private tests `test__synthesize_*` in `tests/test_bench.py`: N, seeding and σ's range; the active fraction within 0.01 at five times for 5 %, 20 % and 100 %; at least 70 % of the rows in front of and inside the image of every camera of a ring rig and of a forward-facing one (78 % measured on both, and on kitchen; 2 to 8 % around the centroid instead) | confirmed |
| Q-182 | §3.4 B-3 ("N from 10⁵ to 3·10⁶, with resizes as at growth events"; "sets D-50's choice; recompilations counted"); D-50; Q-13; Q-168; U-28 | The sizes between 10⁵ and 3·10⁶, the model, the times, the outputs the backward starts from, the warm-up, how the first call is timed, how the sizes are resized, and what B-3 decides, B-1 deciding the CUDA operator (PRD #220's rule) (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | 11 sizes: 10⁵, 1.5·10⁵, 2·10⁵, 3·10⁵, 4·10⁵, 5·10⁵, 7·10⁵, 10⁶, 1.5·10⁶, 2·10⁶ and 3·10⁶, B-1's 0.5M, 1M and 3M among them, so 10 resizes. At each size, eager and then compiled, `impl` given explicitly, never "auto": one first call, timed on the wall clock from a synchronized device to a synchronize after its backward, so it includes compilation and the first launches and never runs inside a timed repetition; 10 warm-up calls; then 50 calls under the stage clock's CUDA events, `slice.kernel` around the forward and `backward` around `torch.autograd.grad` of the five outputs a training iteration differentiates (`means_t`, `opacities_t`, `quats_n`, `scales`, `reg_terms`), with fixed standard-normal upstream gradients, the same for both implementations. The figures are the medians of the forward, of the backward and of the span from the forward's start to the backward's end, the first call, and the peak allocated memory; the times t are uniform in [0, 1] from a seeded generator. Dynamo is reset (`torch.compiler.reset`) and its counters cleared before the first size, and the recompilations are dynamo's unique graphs − 1 over the run (Q-13), set against U-28's limit of 2 as a `limit` budget entry (Q-180). Decision rule, applied by #226 together with B-1: Q-13's "auto" stays the compiled slice on CUDA while its median forward plus backward is at most 1.03 × eager's at every size and its recompilations stay within U-28's limit; otherwise the numbers go to the owner as a request to change "auto"'s mapping, which Q-13 fixes and the code keeps until the owner answers (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed, with the rule's tolerance of 3 % added, where the first wording asked for no slower at every size; no code implements the rule. Applied to the rerun on kitchen after Q-184, it keeps "auto" compiled, as does the CI scene's baseline run, whose compiled slice is at most 1.01 × eager's; the pass rests on Q-184's static inner dims and Q-181's model, both still provisional) | the sizes step evenly in log N with B-1's sizes among them; prefixes of one shuffled model are exactly the resizes of growth events; explicit implementations keep a fallback from timing eager twice; the outputs and gradients of training make the backward the one an iteration runs; up to 5·10⁵ rows both slices sit at the host's launch floor of about 0.3 ms, where a few microseconds decide which is faster, and a loss inside the run-to-run noise is no loss, as Q-173's 3 % has it for a gain | 11 log-spaced sizes; random tensors as in U-28; the first call timed with CUDA events; a shrink to N = 1 as U-28 has (B-3's range starts at 10⁵); no slower than eager at every size (the first wording), which the CI scene's baseline run fails at 10⁵ (0.309 against 0.308 ms) and 7·10⁵ (0.412 against 0.409 ms) while compiled takes 0.54 × eager's time at 3·10⁶ | `test_b3_times_both_slices_at_every_size_and_counts_recompilations` (`tests/test_bench.py`); B-3's rows in the Calibration data table; the CI scene's B-3 baseline (`benchmarks/baselines/nvidia-geforce-rtx-4090/B-3-slice.json`) | confirmed |
