| Q-184 | §2.4 "Kernels" ("compiled: the same code through torch.compile with dynamic shapes, so growth events do not trigger recompilation"); D-50; Q-13; Q-182 ("a lead for #226") | D-50 says the slice is compiled with dynamic shapes but not which dims are dynamic. the body is shared, so changing it would change the eager slice too; B-3's rerun on kitchen (58a2b51, Calibration data) confirmed the probe: eager / compiled 0.323 / 0.308 ms at 10⁵ rows, 0.577 / 0.324 at 10⁶ and 2.289 / 1.238 at 3·10⁶, and the CI baselines give the same 1.242 ms at 3·10⁶ | the norm written elementwise from the four components in the shared body (measured: compiled 1.87 ms at 3·10⁶ but 0.61 ms at 10⁶, no faster than eager, and the eager slice 30 to 50 % slower, 3.37 ms at 3·10⁶); `(q*q).sum(-1)` (no change: 2.46 ms); `dynamic=False` with `maybe_mark_dynamic` on the rows (as fast; but `dynamic=False` reads as "no dynamic shapes", against D-50's wording); automatic dynamic shapes (one recompilation at the first growth event, against D-50) | U-28 (values ≤ 1e-6, gradients ≤ 1e-5 relative, ≤ 2 recompilations over 10 resizes, on plain tensors and through `at_time` on a resized model; | Q-185 | §3.4 B-1 ("time per iteration by stage (temporal slice, rasterization forward and backward, each loss, Adam, statistics, events, data wait) from CUDA events; peak memory"; "full training ≤ 60 min on an RTX 4090"); D-50 ("more than 10% of an iteration"); Q-72 note 5; Q-168 (B-1's bound); Q-180 | #226 fixes B-1's models, configuration, cache and timed iterations (121 to 420, events included) and what it reports, but leaves open: how a stage is summed up and its share computed; how the slice's backward is told apart inside the backward stage; what "the event time" of Q-168's bound is and which case's numbers the bound takes; how B-1 accounts for the stage clock's own cost (about 9 µs of host time per stage, Calibration data, #223); where the active and visible fractions are measured, the trainer exposing no render output; how the copy of the relocation scores is timed; the SH degree of a model given with `--model`; and BENCH.json's cases and figures (from #226: M15 · B-1 (bench train-step) and the D-50 decision) | Each case gets a fresh `Trainer` on CUDA. Iterations 1 to 120 are untimed (the slice's compilation, the frame cache's placement, the first event at 100); peak memory is reset before 121. **Window A**, 121 to 420, under `Clock("cuda")`, read once after 420: per stage over the 297 iterations without an event, the mean, the median, the count, and the share, the stage's mean over the iteration's mean (a ratio of means); the backward stage split at its two points into `backward.losses`, `backward.raster` and `backward.slice`, the last being the slice's backward (the SH concat's backward and the gradients' accumulation end there too); D-50's share is (`slice.kernel` + `backward.slice`) / `iteration`, and the largest over the cases is set against 10 % as a `limit` budget entry (Q-180); per event (200, 300, 400) the `events` stage and the whole iteration. Then, without gradients, the shares of rows that pass the opacity cull (D-32, "active") and that are visible (E6), averaged over window A's 300 images and times, on the model at 420. **Window B**, 421 to 720, without the clock: one pair of CUDA events around each `step()`; its mean over the iterations without an event is the iteration time of a run without the clock, and window A's mean less it is the clock's cost; the event time is the mean of window B's event iterations (500, 600, 700) less that mean, what an event iteration adds, the log read and the validity checks included. The bound is T_it × window B's mean + the default calendar's number of events (246, counted with `calendar_entry`) × that event time, as a `bound` budget entry on the case with the largest N, among equal N the largest bound. The scores' copy is the median of 50 wall-clock copies of an [N] float32 GPU tensor to the CPU, after 10, N being the case's at the end. With `--model`, the model's SH degree replaces 3, and a model of another time normalizer is exit 2. Cases are `{"n", "active"}` (`{"model": "given", "n"}` with `--model`), with `"stage"` or `"iteration"` added for the per-stage and per-event entries (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed; the rationale says what the event time measures: an event at the cap) | the ratio of means is what an iteration's time is made of, the plain reading of D-50's "10% of an iteration"; the points split the backward without a synchronization (U-29's stage-clock case); the clock's cost is host time, so a window without it measures what training costs, and window B runs at the same N (growth reaches n_max at iteration 100) and the same kind of work, where a second run would repeat the setup and warm-up; an event iteration's extra time is what the bound must add per event; measuring the fractions after the window keeps it free of extra synchronizations. The event time is that of an event at the cap: growth reached n_max at the event at 100 (at 200 with 5 % active), so at 500, 600 and 700 it computes its statistics and candidates but adds no rows, and a synthesized model has next to nothing dead to relocate. An event of a default run that adds rows also copies the stored tensors and Adam's two moments, about 2.4 GB at 3M rows, a few ms by memory bandwidth (an estimate, not measured), at most about a second over the default calendar's 146 growth events; the bound leaves it out, as it leaves out real relocation (Q-168). | Q-186 | D-54; Q-175 (an `Evaluator` per device; the warm-up); Q-68; Q-106 and Q-122 (`roma_init`'s `devices`) | Q-175 gives each device a model copy and, for evaluation, an `Evaluator`, but leaves open what becomes of the evaluator the caller passes, whether a repeated entry gets a copy of its own, how `devices` is checked, which work a shard takes, which paths of the temporal slice the warm-up runs, and which devices the CLI passes when it runs on the CPU (from #233: M15 · D-54 sharding of rendering and evaluation) | `devices` is a non-empty sequence of device names: a string, an empty sequence or an entry that isn't a device is a `ValueError` before anything renders, as Q-122 has it for `roma_init`. Each entry is one shard, so a repeated entry is another shard on the same device, as in Q-106. Every shard gets its own model copy, made with the public constructor from the model's tensors and keeping `slice_impl`, also on the model's own device; in `evaluate_images` it also gets `Evaluator(device=entry)`, built by the public constructor, and the evaluator passed is not used. The copies, the evaluators, each shard's once-per-request state (background, cameras, SH concat and temporal index, Q-174) and the warm-up are made in the caller's thread, one shard after another, before any worker thread starts. The warm-up runs the temporal slice once under inference mode on each path the shard's renders take: every row, and, when gsplat slices the index's rows, a gathered copy of every row. Whether the index is on and its bucket width are decided once for the whole request. A shard's jobs come from one shared queue: `render_request`'s groups (timestamp and image size), and `evaluate_images`' timestamps, each rendered and scored on the shard's device. Results come back in request or record order; a shard's exception is raised in the caller's thread once every shard has stopped. In a sharded evaluation, the caller's thread clears `cudnn.allow_tf32` and sets float32 matrix products to "highest" precision once for the whole run, and restores both afterwards, also on an error, so each metric's own save and restore (D-52, Q-60) sees IEEE float32's values throughout (the matrix precision since #387, for ꟻLIP's colour transforms, which are matrix products, Q-60). `ftgs render` with gsplat and `ftgs eval` on CUDA pass `cuda:0` to `cuda:n−1`, n from `torch.cuda.device_count()`, only when n ≥ 2; with one visible CUDA device, or on the CPU (`--backend reference`, or no CUDA), they pass None, so nothing is copied. This refines Q-175's "the CLI passes every visible CUDA device"; `render_request` and `evaluate_images` are unchanged. The worker threads are `openftgs._shards`' runner, which `roma_init` shares (owner answer, 2026-10-19, during the build of PRD #220: changed — the CLI shards only with two or more visible CUDA devices) | The evaluator's device is private, and every shard built the same way keeps the shards alike; a copy per entry is what a repeated entry simulates (U-33's three shards on one GPU); one thread compiling at a time, with Q-13's fallback warning raised in the caller's thread; a decision taken per shard could differ between shards; a shared queue balances groups of unequal cost; the TF32 flag and the matrix precision are process-global, so per-call save and restore across threads would leave them wrong during and after the run; the reference backend renders on the CPU (Q-125). With the owner's answer the CLI copies nothing on one GPU; with several, each shard holds its own model and ꟻLIP loss (LPIPS networks until revision 549o), which no number depends on. Put to the owner during #233; answered on 2026-10-19 (see the decision) | the first shard on the model's (or evaluator's) device reusing it, which saves one copy; a fixed round-robin split of the jobs; warming every path whatever the request needs, which can add a graph toward dynamo's recompile limit (Q-13); rendering every group first and scoring after, which holds every render at once | `test_render_request_sharded_on_the_cpu_is_bitwise_unsharded`, `test_render_request_warms_each_shard_in_the_caller_thread_first`, `test_render_request_refuses_malformed_devices`, `test_render_request_raises_a_shards_error_in_the_caller`, `test_evaluate_images_sharded_on_the_cpu_equals_unsharded`, `test_sharded_evaluation_clears_tf32_once_and_restores_it`, `test_sharded_evaluation_names_the_renders_that_are_not_finite`, `test_evaluate_images_refuses_malformed_devices` (`tests/test_render.py`, `tests/test_eval.py`); the GPU tests with `["cuda:0"] * 3`, `test_render_request_sharded_on_one_gpu_matches_unsharded` and `test_evaluate_images_sharded_on_one_gpu_equals_unsharded`; the CLI's device tests (`tests/test_cli.py`) | changed (#233) |

relocation_score (`opacity`, `g_sum`, `count`; RelocConfig fields → `score` [N]); spatial_scale (`world_to_camera` [C,4,4], `train` [C]; `factor` → `S`); growth_decisions (`g` [N], `scales` [N,3]; `spatial_scale`, `grad_threshold`, `split_scale`, `n_max` → `clone`, `split` [N] bool); owner decision, 2026-10-26, #376, Q-255: the `losses` answers carry `flip`, ꟻLIP, in place of `lpips_vgg`, at §3.3's 1e-5 relative, and ꟻLIP is the total's perceptual term at the query's λ_perc; the queries have no `lpips` parameter, and an adapter without ꟻLIP answers them `not_comparable` (#390) | One file per kind keeps a crash in one kind from sinking the others; `tests/harness/test_adapter.py` (each kind against the direct call); since #390, `tests/harness/test_adapter_forward.py`'s ꟻLIP cases and `tests/harness/test_harness_l2.py`'s not-comparable and schema-violation cases without `flip` | changed (#390) |
| Q-208 | §3.3 L0 ("1e-6 relative"); the L2 tolerance column | "Relative" isn't defined where values are near 0, and a bare number has no stated meaning (from #279: M16 · Differential harness) | A bare number is absolute. "Relative" means \|a − b\| ≤ tol · max(\|a\|, \|b\|, f · M), elementwise, where M = max(‖a‖∞, ‖b‖∞) over the compared array, so the verdict doesn't depend on which implementation is A. f = 1 for signed quantities, which pass through 0 and which a faithful float32 implementation holds only to its rounding at the array's scale, about M · 2⁻²⁴: L0's `position`, `velocity`, `rotation`, `sh` and `time_center` (an implementation may store μt as float32 normalized time, positions in a normalised scene frame or the DC coefficient as RGB) and L2's `means_t`. f = 1e-3 for positive quantities, whose relative error stays elementwise in log-domain storage: L0's `scale`, `duration` and `opacity`, and L2's other relative answers (the loss terms, `rates`, `opacity_new`, `scales_new`, `grad_norm`, `S`). L0 compares the metadata exactly (`sh_degree`, τ₀, τ₁, `time_unit` and the background of D-84, black when absent, since a round trip that loses the background changes what the colours mean; added after the milestone review), opacity after the [10⁻⁷, 1 − 10⁻⁷] clamp and quaternions up to sign. The generators keep velocity magnitudes near 1, so the absolute 1e-6 of `velocity_init` stays above float32 rounding, with little headroom (float32 and float64 differ by up to 7.8 · 10⁻⁷ on its queries) (owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: changed from a 1e-3 · ‖b‖∞ floor for every field but `time_center`, under which an exact float64 E1 failed 431 of 600 L2 `motion` queries against openftgs's float32 answers; #312) | An elementwise relative test fails on values that pass through 0, such as positions and time centres; a floor below float32 rounding at the array's scale fails faithful implementations of signed quantities; the largest-magnitude floor on positive fields would pass a 10⁻⁴ error in their small entries | pure elementwise relative; relative to the array's maximum only, for every field; a 1e-3 floor for every field but `time_center` (the provisional decision); a fixed floor between 1e-2 and 1e-1 (fragile where the largest entry is close to the typical one) | the L2 fault test (answers perturbed by 2× the tolerance fail, by 0.5× pass); an exact float64 E1 passes L2 `motion` against openftgs; a position round trip through a normalised scene frame in float32 passes L0; zeroing a signed field's entries below 10⁻² · M fails; a 10⁻⁴ error in only the smallest durations fails L0 | changed (#312) |
| Q-209 | §3.3 L0 (twenty models, random and trained), L1 (items, probes P1 to P10), L2 (edge values) | The spec gives the coverage but not the cases (from #279: M16 · Differential harness) | Case sizes from the run settings (Q-203). L0: trained models from the settings first, then seeded random models up to the size (varied N, SH degree 0 to 3, a single-timestamp model, saturated opacities, edge scales and durations); the report records how many were trained; each round trip also renders a 4-item request before and after. L1 main case: one model (trained if given, otherwise random, N ≈ 1,000); a ring rig of 8 "training" cameras at 128×96 and 20 frames; at least 200 items covering training cameras, novel cameras between them on the rig's circle, times on frames, between frames, at τ₀ and τ₁, and 10 % outside [τ₀, τ₁]; the rig is fitted to the model (8 cameras at three times the 90th-percentile distance of the positions from their median, a radius of at least 1). Each L0 check records whether its model was trained; `scripts/harness_s9.py` gives L0 two trained models, 700-iteration runs of S-9 (set during the build). L1 probes: one or two primitives each, a 21-item sweep at 64×48; the sweeps of P4, P6, P7 and P8 keep their samples outside Q-39's margins around E6's cutoffs; P10 is P2 swept outside [τ₀, τ₁]. L2: 4 seeded random queries per kind plus edge queries; relocation_sampling with 20 live targets and 50 × 200 draws, each implementation's pooled counts tested by χ² goodness of fit against the scores and A against B by homogeneity, all with p > 0.001; relocation_score inputs avoid σ within 1e-6 of `dead_opacity` (owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: confirmed) | A sweep sample on a cutoff would fail any pair of rasterizers by rounding alone; pooled counts against the known scores test what D-15 says | trained models made by the harness through short `train` runs; a χ² test of A against B only | the CPU level tests; L1 reference against gsplat | confirmed |
| Q-210 | §3.3 L3 steps 1 to 6; D-54; E16 | Open: the A′ seeds, which model renders, the background, α and the tested metric family, the power formula, the distribution statistic, the multiple-test rule for flags, trace iterations for a shortened run, a trace check's pass rule (from #279: M16 · Differential harness) | Seeds 0…K−1 for A and B, K…2K−1 for A′. One adapter process per run, round robin over the settings' GPUs with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` set to one GPU; serial without GPUs listed. The held-out request is built from the manifest's test split with `harness_config`'s background (black when absent); the run's native model renders it and its FTGS-IF export feeds the distributions. PSNR, SSIM₁, SSIM₂ and ꟻLIP are tested (owner decision, 2026-10-26, #376, after revision 549o: ꟻLIP in place of LPIPS-Alex and LPIPS-VGG, so Holm corrects across four metrics × scenes); DSSIM is reported only. Welch, and TOST as two one-sided Welch tests, at α = 0.05 with Holm across metrics × scenes; the K for 80 % power by the normal approximation refined with t quantiles at df = 2K − 2. Distributions: the statistic d(A, B) − d(A, A′) on pooled samples for KS and Wasserstein-1, an exact permutation test over the C(2K, K) splits of B ∪ A′; a flag (raw p < 0.01) is a failed check, Holm-corrected at 0.01 across quantities × distances × scenes; when K is too small for any flag to be reachable the check is `inconclusive`, naming the smallest reachable p and the K needed (at K = 5 the smallest p is 1/252 ≈ 0.004, above Holm's first threshold of 0.0007 over 14 tests). Each run's samples of a quantity are subsampled to 20,000 without replacement, seeded, before KS and W1. Traces: B's median is also compared with the spread of A's and A′'s seeds, reported in the check's localization, not judged. Each metric check reports every seed's value, A′'s included (set during the build). Traces: loss terms and N at 1k, 7k, 15k and 30k scaled by `optim.iterations`/30,000, the last row at or before each target, reported by median and spread, failing only on a missing or non-finite row (owner answer on the flags, 2026-10-20, while planning; owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: Holm kept although no flag is reachable at K = 5, and a change to §3.3 L3 step 5 requested, #308, made in revision 549m; owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: the rest confirmed) | Uncorrected flags would give about 13 % false flags per scene against a B that differs from A only by GPU non-determinism; the proportional scaling follows §3.2's shared setup; rendering the native model keeps converter faults in L0 | uncorrected flags; flags reported without failing; a default K of 7; rendering the exported FTGS-IF file | L3 plumbing with the replay adapter, Holm recomputed across the four metrics × scenes (`tests/harness/test_harness_l3.py`); the end-to-end test | changed (#391) |
| Q-211 | §3.3 "Report format"; Q-63 | Open: the status vocabulary, how a level's summary is formed, whether `inconclusive` and `not comparable` fail, check IDs, non-finite numbers in JSON, symptom thresholds, the choice of trend model (from #279: M16 · Differential harness) | Statuses `pass`, `fail`, `inconclusive`, `not_comparable`. A level is `fail` if any check failed, otherwise `inconclusive` if any check was, otherwise `pass` if any check passed, otherwise `not_comparable`, so a level with nothing compared doesn't read as passed (owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: the row confirmed with this change); `not_comparable` checks are counted and listed but fail nothing, so with K = 5 L3 reads `inconclusive`. Exit 0 means no failed check, and the milestone's "passes every level" is read the same way (owner answer, 2026-10-20, after the build: confirmed, with a matching change to §3.3 requested, #308, made in revision 549m). Check IDs such as `L0.roundtrip.m07`, `L0.render.m07`, `L0.info.A`, `L1.render.main.r0123`, `L1.render.P2.r0007`, `L2.forward.motion.q0003`, `L3.metric.s9.psnr`, `L3.dist.s9.speed.ks`, `L3.trace.s9.7k`. Non-finite values are written as null (`allow_nan=False`, as Q-63); an identical render's PSNR is null with an "inf" note and counts as a pass. L1's per-item measures use the unclamped renders with peak 1. Symptoms are measured from the alpha centroid offset (px), second moments (scale change), alpha mass (missing or extra primitive), the mean colour under the alpha mask (colour shift; view-dependent for P5), the peak and width of the alpha mass against time (temporal shift, widening or narrowing), a border band, NaN/Inf, and crashes and schema violations from the adapter outcome. The trend is the constant, linear or quadratic least-squares fit with the lowest BIC, residuals within 0.1 % of the data's largest magnitude counting as exact (so a negligible slope doesn't win), reported as a string in `localization.trend` ("linear in \|τ − time_center\|", as §3.3's example) with its coefficients in `localization.trend_coefficients`. Pattern thresholds, checked in this order: non-finite values; B's alpha mass below 0.8× A's (missing primitive) or above 1.25× (extra primitive); an alpha-centroid offset above 0.2 px (offset); a spread off by more than 3 % (scale change); more than 80 % of Σ\|Δ\| within 3 px of the border (border band); \|Δα\| above 0.01 where A's α > 0.9 (opacity saturation); the alpha's gradient energy below 0.85× A's (blur); otherwise a colour shift. A time sweep shows a temporal pattern when its alpha-mass profile's centre moves by more than 1 % of the sweep or its width by more than 2 %, unless B's profile is A's scaled within 10 %; P5's colour shift is view-dependent when its difference varies by more than 30 % of its largest value. Checks whose discrepancy has no image form (most L2 kinds, L3's statistics) carry no pattern. Checks add `pattern` and `note` beside §3.3's keys; every failed render item gets its A, B and \|A − B\| images; `summary` lists the levels that ran (set during the build) | An inconclusive verdict is not a failed check; clamping would hide differences above 1 in renders the formats store unclamped; BIC penalizes the extra terms that would otherwise always win | exit 1 on inconclusive; Infinity in the JSON; the best fit by R² | the report tests; the symptom tests | confirmed |

Every parallel run ends with S-4, and the CPUs stay saturated: user and system time were 8,000 to 9,300 s per run, and 29 to 33 threads were runnable on average. | Q-223 | §2.4 rule 2; §2.4 "Where the time goes", perceptual-loss row ("channels-last inputs; … cuDNN autotuning and channels-last weights measured by B-4 and left off", which #326 overtakes; revision 549n states this row's decision there, #331); D-52; Q-173 (changed, #326); Q-190; `PerfConfig.perc_precision`, `PerfConfig.deterministic`; E16 | Q-173's change adopts contiguous NCHW inputs with cuDNN autotuning for training's perceptual loss, a combination B-4's comparisons measured only in TF32 (#326 leaves precisions out of scope, D-52). The trainer chooses its network once, from `PerfConfig`: NCHW inputs under "ieee", and under "tf32" when the run autotunes; the channels-last view of a render under "tf32" with `deterministic`, and always under "bf16" (`train._perceptual_network`; since #388 `train._lpips_network`, for LPIPS's backbones only until #392, while D-89's networks take the render's view until B-4's rerun, Q-251). The NCHW network is a private subclass of `LPIPSLoss` in `openftgs.train` whose `forward` copies the two [..., H, W, 3] images so that `LPIPSLoss`'s batch-first views of them are contiguous NCHW, then calls `LPIPSLoss.forward`; `LPIPSLoss` is unchanged, so the evaluator's networks and the harness's keep the channels-last view. B-4 adds the comparison `autotuning_and_nchw_inputs` (TF32: channels-last inputs, autotuning off → NCHW inputs, autotuning on), which replaces `autotuning` in its setup's `turn_on` (`autotuning` stays reported); its setup's `training_layout` becomes the autotuned layout per precision, `{"ieee": "nchw", "tf32": "nchw", "bf16": "channels_last_inputs"}`; its other comparisons and its deviations keep Q-190's baselines, the path training ran before #326. The trainer's NCHW term is held to the channels-last one within 1e-5 relative in value and 1e-4 in relative L2 of the prediction's gradient in IEEE float32 (the CPU's float32 included), and within 1e-3 and 0.1 in TF32 on CUDA (owner decision, 2026-10-26, #376 (#380), Q-251: changed — since #394 each of D-89's networks takes the layout and the autotuning that B-4's recorded rerun chose for it, Q-273's table, vgg16_oxford's being this LPIPS-VGG pattern and alexnet_bvlc's NCHW everywhere; the decision above is that of the trainer before revision 549o) | B-4's medians (Calibration data, 2026-10-19), against each precision's path before #326 (channels-last inputs, autotuning off): TF32 with NCHW inputs and autotuning +7.2 % / +3.9 %, but NCHW without autotuning −4.3 % / −4.9 % (105.91 against 101.55 ms, 157.98 against 150.59 ms), so a deterministic TF32 run keeps channels-last; IEEE with NCHW inputs +13.5 % / +16.2 % without autotuning (99.90 against 115.48 ms, 151.50 against 180.74 ms) and +19.8 % / +23.0 % with it (92.58, 139.19 ms); under bf16 NCHW inputs are slower than its old path with autotuning (72.30 against 68.03 ms, 110.32 against 105.09 ms), while channels-last with autotuning gains +3.5 % / +5.8 % (65.65, 99.03 ms). B-4's NCHW cases start from an NCHW leaf, so they leave out the trainer's copy and its backward; B-1's rerun times the whole path. The copy is exact; the convolutions then take other cuDNN kernels, whose results differ by rounding: 1e-8 between layouts on the CPU (U-9's mapping test), and TF32's 10-bit mantissa, about 2·10⁻⁵ in value and 3 % in gradient L2 from IEEE float32 in B-4, bounds two TF32 kernels' difference. A subclass in `openftgs.train` keeps every other caller of `LPIPSLoss` as it was, as `_Bf16LPIPSLoss` does (Q-172). Evaluation runs outside training's flags, without autotuning, and its configurations (IEEE, channels-last) share none with the trainer's, which differ in layout, TF32 flag or dtype. Autotuning picks its algorithms at the first iteration, when N is small; B-4 measured up to 13.7 GiB peak allocated with NCHW inputs and autotuning, so B-1's rerun records peak GPU memory | NCHW inputs under every precision and setting, as the issue words it, which B-4's medians make 5 to 6 % slower under bf16 and 4 to 5 % slower in a deterministic TF32 run; NCHW inputs whenever the precision is float32, deterministic or not; a private class attribute on `LPIPSLoss` read by its `forward` (the first wording on this branch); an attribute set on the instance by the trainer; a constructor keyword (a change to §2.1's `LPIPSLoss` signature); NCHW inputs in `LPIPSLoss` for every caller, evaluation included | `test_the_trainers_perceptual_network_gets_nchw_inputs_where_b4_measured_them_faster`, `test_a_float32_steps_perceptual_term_takes_nchw_inputs_within_rounding_of_channels_last` and `test_cudnn_autotuning_during_a_step_is_on_unless_deterministic` (`tests/test_train.py`); B-4's comparison test (`tests/test_bench.py`); B-4's and B-1's reruns (Calibration data, #326); since #394 Q-273's tests | changed (#394) |

a version-1 file's LPIPS-Alex, LPIPS-VGG and lpips_vgg_raw), in the `entire` and `dynamic` blocks; a finding also when a value is null on one side and a number on the other (null on both is a null difference), and for a block, a metric or an image path present in one file only; every difference is written to COMPARISON.json either way, with each file's METRICS.json version under `versions`; against a version-1 file, the metrics one version holds alone (ꟻLIP; the three LPIPS values) are compared nowhere, listed once under `not_compared` with no finding (Q-266), and a given file of another version is exit 2 (owner answer, 2026-10-21, #332: changed from 0.001 for the other metrics, PSNR-only per-image checks and one-sided values skipped; owner decision, 2026-10-26, #376, Q-256: the comparison reads versions 1 and 2 and says which it read) | both values come from the same forward pass, and evaluation runs with cuDNN autotuning and TF32 off, so they agree to the last bits on one machine (every M17 difference was exactly 0) and to far below 10⁻⁴ across machines; 0.001 was about kitchen's seed spread (`default` against `seed1`: SSIM₁ 0.00098, LPIPS-Alex 0.0015, LPIPS-VGG 0.0013) while 0.01 dB is a fifth of PSNR's (0.050 dB), so 10⁻⁴ puts the other metrics on PSNR's footing; a one-sided value, block, metric or image is the largest possible disagreement | 0.001 (the first provisional decision); no SSIM or LPIPS findings (PSNR only); a relative bound; exact equality (fails on last-bit differences across GPUs or library versions) | the comparison test of `tests/test_view_renders.py` (2·10⁻⁴ is a finding, 5·10⁻⁵ isn't, per scene and per image; null against a number; one-sided block, metric and image; a version-2 run against a version-1 file, Q-266's, and other versions refused); the M17 comparisons rewritten in #333 | changed (#387) |
| Q-225 | D-51; §2.4 "Data pipeline" ("half the GPU memory that is free after a warm-up iteration, minus what growth to N_max will add"); Q-99 (changed, #325); Q-189 (B-6's estimate and `margin_used`) | Q-99's change (owner, 2026-10-21) makes "auto" also reserve the renderer's and the losses' working memory at N_max and the scene's image size before choosing the GPU, but doesn't say how that reserve is estimated, where it enters the rule, or how B-6's estimate and `margin_used` follow (from #325: Frame cache "auto" leaves no room for the renderer at N_max (Q-99, D-51)) | Measured, at the point where "auto" decides (on CUDA after the iteration that follows the first at the last training image size to appear, the second for one size, Q-232, #348; after the second whatever the sizes from #341; after the first until then): the reserve is the memory PyTorch's allocator then holds unused, reserved − allocated, which is the warm-up iteration's working memory (the render's buffers, the losses' with the perceptual network at full size (LPIPS-VGG's in the figures this row records, measured before revision 549o), the gradients) that the caching allocator keeps. The cache goes to the GPU when `size_of` ≤ ½·(free − reserve) − growth, free and growth as Q-99 has them (free = `mem_get_info`'s free + reserved − allocated); so the half is taken of `mem_get_info`'s free memory alone. What the working memory grows by from the warm-up's N to N_max is left to the other half. Memory reserved before the first iteration (an earlier trainer in the same process, initialization on the GPU) counts in the reserve, which only makes the rule more conservative; with PyTorch's caching turned off (`PYTORCH_NO_CUDA_MEMORY_CACHING=1`) or an allocator configuration that releases the warm-up's blocks, the reserve shrinks and the rule loosens towards Q-99's earlier one. cuDNN autotuning (#326) does the same at a process's first iteration, measured on studio's training (Calibration data, 2026-10-22): the iteration ends with a reserve of 1.29 GB where it keeps 8.99 GB without autotuning, so a decision after it sets aside little of the working memory and memory-mapped studio's cache by 0.18 GB; hence the second iteration, after which the allocator holds the working memory again (8.54 GB on studio, below). Cameras of different image sizes re-tune at each new size (Q-232). B-6 follows the rule, its probe taking as many steps as "auto" waits (#341, #348): `auto_limit_bytes` is ½·(free − reserve) − growth; `beyond_estimate_bytes` = peak reserved − the probe's reserved − the cache − growth, what the run took beyond the cache, growth and reserve; `margin_used` = that over ½·(free − reserve), the half the rule leaves. B-6's figures and cases are otherwise unchanged; the reserve is `auto_reserved_bytes` − `auto_allocated_bytes` (owner decision, 2026-10-22, #340: changed — "auto" decides after the second CUDA iteration, not the first, with the reserve and the rule otherwise as above; B-6's probe takes two steps to match. Under cuDNN autotuning (#326) the first iteration empties the allocator: a diagnostic of the default configuration with the frame cache off (a scratch script, not committed, at 6dab471) read a reserve of 1.35 GB after iteration 1 on studio (83 device frees) and 8.54 after iterations 2 to 6, against 9.05 from iteration 1 on without autotuning; on kitchen 0.59, then 5.66 and 5.71. The limits after iteration 2, 4.71 on studio against its 8.58 GB cache and about 6.3 on kitchen against kitchen60's 4.69, put studio's cache in mmap with 3.9 GB to spare and keep kitchen60's and CI's on the GPU. The warm-up's peak reserved memory, the alternative #340 raised, counts autotuning's trial workspaces (14.00 and 15.53 GB peaks), which would give kitchen a limit of 1.51 and skip CI's GPU case; the rule, Q-99's wording and Q-189's probe are set in #341) | B-6's recorded figures: the peak allocated memory less the probe's allocated memory and the cache is 5.69 and 5.90 GB on kitchen60 (1352×1014) at 1M and 3M rows and 8.53 and 8.74 GB on studio (1920×1080), about 4.1 KB per pixel in both scenes and 0.1 GB per million rows at 20 % active; the probe's reserved − allocated is the same working memory (6.05 and 5.82 GB; 9.13 and 9.29 GB), which Q-99 counted as free and left to the ½ margin a second time. Measured, the reserve follows the configuration (λ_perc = 0, the loss precision, the backend) and the image size with no constants. On the recorded figures the rule gives studio at 3M a limit of 6.21 GB against its 8.58 GB cache (mmap, as #325 requires) and kitchen60 at 3M 7.93 GB against 4.69 GB (the GPU, 3.2 GB to spare; the CI scene's 60-frame conversion, the same size, 7.99 GB in its stored baseline) | a formula a·H·W + b·N_max calibrated on B-6 (about 4.1 KB per pixel and 0.1 KB per row at 20 % active), which fixes the default losses and two scenes in constants; ½·free − growth − reserve, the whole reserve after halving, which leaves CI's 60-frame GPU case 0.39 GB under its limit (5.08 GB against 4.69), within run-to-run noise of being skipped, which fails the baseline comparison; the warm-up iteration's peak from `reset_peak_memory_stats`, a global side effect on the caller's own peak; scaling the reserve to the largest camera's image when the cameras' sizes differ | B-6's `margin_used` on studio and kitchen60, at most 1 wherever "auto" chooses the GPU; studio's default training at #326's code and again at #341's, which must not run out of GPU memory (the owner, #325); `test__step_chooses_the_placement_from_the_second_iterations_allocator` and, for two sizes, `test__step_chooses_the_placement_after_every_image_size_has_run`; `test__fits_on_gpu_reserves_the_working_memory` at studio's figures | changed (#341) |

| Q-246 | §3.4, the CI paragraph ("fails when a budgeted figure gets more than 10% worse than its stored baseline"); §3.4 B-3; Q-170 (the figures compared); Q-196 (the comparison and the refresh); Q-179 (no runner) | One B-3 case takes one of two levels from run to run, so at 1.10 × its baseline it fails in a share of clean runs with no code change. The compiled slice at 10⁶ rows, the last size before the compiled times start growing with N (0.325 ms at 7·10⁵ and 0.324 at 10⁶ in the refreshed baselines, 0.510 at 1.5·10⁶), measures about 0.32 ms or 0.36 to 0.45 ms: 0.447 (ci-1), 0.328 (ci-2), 0.430 (ci-369-1, an excluded run), 0.322, 0.364 and 0.324 (ci-369-2 to 4). Against the refreshed 0.324 ms it fails 2 of the 5 clean CI runs. In ci-1 the compiled 7·10⁵ case went high with it (0.412) while 5·10⁵ and 1.5·10⁶ stayed normal; the eager 10⁶ case, which B-3 times between the two (eager then compiled at each N), stayed normal (0.614 against 0.605), and in ci-369-3 only the compiled 10⁶ case was slow, so the slow level may belong to the compiled path rather than to the whole run. #377's runs (Calibration data, 2026-10-25) superseded this reading: the slow level is the host's, covers every case whose time the host sets (both implementations up to 5·10⁵ rows, compiled 7·10⁵ and 10⁶), comes and goes within a process and lasts 0.15 s to more than 14 s, also off CPU 5 (from #371: Q-n candidates: B-3's compiled slice at 10⁶ rows, at two levels from run to run, against §3.4's 10 % rule (Q-170, Q-196)) | Keep §3.4's 10 % and the baselines #369 refreshed, and judge B-3 only where the GPU sets the time: Q-170 judges a B-3 case only when its baseline's `forward_backward_s` is at least 1.4 × the smallest `forward_backward_s` of the same implementation in that baseline file (on the RTX 4090, eager from 10⁶ rows and compiled from 1.5·10⁶, 7 of the 22 cases); the other B-3 cases are reported, not judged (Q-196); a refresh takes the median of runs for every B-3 case, judged or not (Q-245); a count of the kernels each call launches guards the slice's launch overhead instead of the dropped times. The code follows in #379 (owner answer, 2026-10-26, on #377, from #377's experiment: 22 runs, a probe and a pinned session. Earlier: owner answer, 2026-10-25, on #371: confirmed, with the experiment to run before a GPU runner is registered (Q-179) and the fix to choose from its result; amended during the build of #371: the floor's 125 µs corrected to 123 µs, and three statements corrected from the B-3 files and the order of B-3's cases) | The 10 % is the spec's. Under Q-179 the GPU job runs only by hand and no runner is registered, so nothing can fail by chance yet; but the failure rate is known, so the case is settled before a runner exists rather than after a false failure. #377 settled it: separated blocks don't escape the slow level, keeping the job off CPU 5's core doesn't remove it, and the 7 cases where the GPU sets the time spread at most 2.9 % over 27 clean runs, none failing, against 11 of 27 failing when every case is judged | **Ruled out:** an absolute floor, which to cover the slow level needs at least 123 µs (0.447 − 0.324 ms), about 40 % of every 0.31 ms case, so it would hide real slowdowns in two-thirds of B-3; more repetitions or a longer window alone, since whole 50-call medians shift, forward and backward, for seconds at a time. **From the experiment:** if the slow level comes and goes within a session, time each case in several separated blocks and keep the lowest median (a change to how B-3 measures a case, within the spec; it changes Q-168's timing, which Q-170 judges); if it holds for a whole run, narrow which B-3 cases Q-170 compares, for example only the sizes past the flat region for both implementations (a change to Q-170, not to §3.4: B-3 has no budget, and it was Q-170 that extended the rule to its medians; | Q-258 | §2.3's pretrained-trunk row ("pinned by SHA-256", "downloaded at first use, never redistributed") and its note on weights; D-89's weights column; #380 ("into torch's hub cache", "cached as safetensors"); Q-253 | The spec and the PRD say where the six files are downloaded and that they are checked, but not the names they are cached under, what is kept of a Caffe file, how the converted file is checked, or what becomes of a file that fails its check; CI's cache of the weights (Q-253) and B-4's missing-weight check (#389) depend on the names (from #384: Trunk weights: download, hash check, Caffe conversion and DINO checkpoints) | Each publisher's file is cached at `torch.hub.get_dir()/checkpoints/<the URL's file name>`, torch.hub's own convention and RoMa's (`roma_weight_files`), and checked against its byte count and SHA-256 (source-log row 424) before every use. A download goes to a temporary file beside it and is moved into place only once checked, so a failed, truncated or altered download leaves nothing behind. A cached file that fails its check is refused with an OSError naming it, and left in place for the user to delete, never overwritten. A Caffe file is converted once into `openftgs-<perc_net>.safetensors` in the same folder: only the convolutions D-89's distance reads (weight and bias under Caffe's layer names), float32 as published, with the source's file name and SHA-256 in the file's metadata; the source stays cached beside it. The converted file is checked at every load: its tensors' names, float32 dtype and shapes against the publishers' layer table, then the SHA-256 of their data (little-endian float32, each layer's weight then bias, in the network's order) against the value pinned in the code (source log 442), so the check doesn't depend on safetensors' serialization; one that fails, or that safetensors can't read, is refused in the same way. Unstated: how cuDNN autotuning, one process-global flag for a whole iteration, follows a choice made per network, and what it is without a network; which layout a network takes under "ieee" and "bf16" and in a deterministic run, which B-4 times but whose comparisons the rule doesn't read; and how the trainer's network gets NCHW inputs while the evaluator's and the harness's keep the render's view (from #394: B-4's recorded rerun and each network's layout and autotuning) | **Autotuning** is on in every training iteration that isn't deterministic for vgg16_oxford and alexnet_bvlc. Their adopted option is autotuning with NCHW inputs: vgg16_oxford +4.69 % / +4.71 % on the CI scene and +3.36 % / +3.03 % on kitchen; alexnet_bvlc +15.03 % / +18.51 % and +15.42 % / +16.71 %, the largest of its three, which all meet. For flip, haarpsi and the four DINO networks, none of whose options meets the rule, the flag stays the caller's (Q-73), except where SSIM runs through its PyTorch fallback on CUDA, which autotuning made 10.9 % / 10.3 % and 6.7 % / 9.4 % faster in B-4's two runs: there it is on whatever the network, §2.4 rule 2 read on the fallback's own gain, the flag being one for the whole iteration (owner answer, 2026-10-26, during the #380 build's review: autotuning on whenever the SSIM fallback runs on CUDA, whatever the network, confirmed; and both of the fallback's triggers, fused-ssim missing or a window or σ other than E7's (Q-42), confirmed). The fallback runs, as `losses.ssim` chooses it (Q-42), on a CUDA device with λ_ssim > 0 where fused-ssim doesn't import (Q-2), and also for a window or σ other than the ones fused-ssim hardcodes, the trainer's reading of the answer (provisional; `train._ssim_fallback_on_cuda`). Without a perceptual network (λ_perc = 0) it is on, as in every iteration since #326: the only cuDNN convolutions are then the SSIM fallback's (without fused-ssim). **Layout**: under "tf32" with autotuning, the adopted option's. Otherwise NCHW inputs where B-4's medians at that precision and autotuning make NCHW at least 3 % faster than the render's view at both sizes in both runs, else the render's channels-last view. So vgg16_oxford takes NCHW under "ieee" (+10.3 to +16.0 %, with and without autotuning) and under autotuned "tf32", and the render's view in a deterministic "tf32" run (NCHW −0.9 to −4.7 %) and under "bf16" (−6.9 to −12.3 %): LPIPS-VGG's pattern (Q-223). alexnet_bvlc takes NCHW under every precision, autotuned or not (+3.5 to +88.4 %). The other six take the render's view everywhere, no pair reaching 3 % in both runs. **Channels-last weights**, timed for the two Caffe trunks as a third layout since the #380 build's review (Q-269, the owner's answer), follow the same reading: they go on where they are at least 3 % faster than the render's view with NCHW weights at both sizes in both runs, at that precision and autotuning, and faster than the layout chosen above; B-4's two runs at 1f4c451 put them within −1.4 to +1.0 % everywhere, and alexnet_bvlc's autotuning with them (+6.1 to +9.9 % over the plain path) below its adopted option (+14.9 to +18.5 %), so no network takes them (`train._CHANNELS_LAST_WEIGHTS`, empty; Calibration data, #380's review). **The copy**: a private `PerceptualLoss` subclass in `openftgs.train` (`_NCHWPerceptualLoss`; `_Bf16NCHWPerceptualLoss` for alexnet_bvlc under "bf16", the copy inside the autocast) copies both [..., H, W, 3] images so that the batch-first views `PerceptualLoss.forward` takes are contiguous NCHW, as #326's LPIPS class did; `PerceptualLoss` is unchanged, so the evaluator's and the harness's networks keep the render's view. The NCHW term is held to the render's view within 1e-5 relative in value and, in the prediction's gradient's relative L2, within 1e-3 in IEEE float32 and 0.1 in TF32 on CUDA. B-4's setup reports the trainer's choice per network (`training_autotuning`, `training_layout`) | §2.4's row; TF32 is training's default precision, and the comparisons Q-272's rule reads are TF32's; the other precisions and the deterministic path keep the rule's threshold and its two runs, applied to the one choice left open there, the layout at the network's autotuning. Under autotuned TF32 a reading of layout against layout would deny vgg16_oxford the NCHW inputs of its adopted option (+1.9 % on kitchen at 1352×1014 against channels-last with autotuning), which is why the two readings are kept apart. Without a network the SSIM fallback is the only cuDNN user, and its own comparison meets the rule. On the trainer test's dim render, each layout's float32 gradient of vgg16_oxford lies up to 1.6·10⁻⁴ in relative L2 from float64's (a probe at #394), above Q-223's 1e-4 for LPIPS-VGG, hence 1e-3 | the flag left the caller's for those six networks even when the SSIM fallback runs on CUDA (this row's decision before the owner's answer), B-4 having measured the fallback's gain alone, never beside a network; the fallback read as fused-ssim not importing only, whatever the window; autotuning on for every network, the interim (Q-251); off without a network; per-precision layouts from one run's medians; NCHW for vgg16_oxford in a deterministic TF32 run | `test_each_perceptual_network_takes_the_layout_and_autotuning_b4_chose` (since #380's review also the weights' memory layout) and `test_a_float32_steps_nchw_perceptual_term_is_within_rounding_of_the_renders_view` (`tests/test_train.py`); `test_cudnn_autotuning_during_a_step_is_on_unless_deterministic` without a network; `test_cudnn_autotuning_is_on_whenever_the_ssim_fallback_runs_on_cuda` with ꟻLIP, fused-ssim stood in for or made unimportable; B-4's setup test (`tests/test_bench.py`); B-1's rerun after #394 (Calibration data) | provisional |

## Calibration data

Measured values for the thresholds and budgets the spec calls calibrated (glossary: "Calibrated threshold"), and the calibration runs of M11, M15 and M17. Each row records one measurement; the spec's criteria are never loosened here, and what becomes a spec change is the owner's decision. `<m15-runs>` in a row is the folder outside the repository where M15's runs were recorded, with #350's kitchen60-crops (`docs/modules/cli/benchmarks.md`).
